## Open Issues

_**One opened and none closed on 2026-08-30 by Session 131b, and the decision it was asked to take went five ways to one.** The handoff asked whether the six pre-convention files that open the live database owe premise assertions. Answered per file rather than in bulk: **four owed one and had none**, one owed **the marker and never the premise**, and one owes nothing. `test_schema_drift.py` is the strongest and was settled by driving rather than reading — its whole output is `diff == []`, and with `FROZEN_TABLES` widened to cover all thirteen mapped tables (the blindfold that constant's own docstring warns about) `compare_metadata` returns `[]` as well, so the guard could certify a comparison it was no longer making._

_**The exemption is earned now rather than granted, which is what makes the decision recorded rather than remembered.** Rule 2's sweep accepts a file off the `_live` glob that **marks a premise**, so `PRE_CONVENTION` shrank 6 → 1 by five files holding the property instead of by five names being trusted. Driven both ways: dropping the new clause reports exactly the five, and stripping one file's marker reports exactly that file. The falsifications are the part worth carrying — under the blindfold the two new premise tests go red and `test_models_match_migrated_schema` stays **green**, and over an emptied `log_entries` the new premise goes red while `test_every_stored_source_is_declared` stays green. The vacuous pass, demonstrated twice._

_`SNAG-TEST-002` is the opening, and it is the exemption's stated cost: `test_snag_claims.py` owes no marker because its premises are enforced in the producer — `query_one`'s every way of not-knowing is `unknown` rather than `match` — and **18 of 18** registered checks are driven to that branch by a test in the class that names them, across 60 such assertions. Nothing enforces it for a nineteenth._

_Previously: **One closed and none opened on 2026-08-30 by Session 130, and what the entry could not find by bisecting was the hour it was run at.** `SNAG-TRAY-010` is **fixed**: `tests/test_desktop_store_live.py` supplies two leaves and says so in its own docstring, and misses a third — `DndManager.is_active` calls `datetime.now()` itself, so the DND gate reads the **real** wall clock whatever clock the notifier was handed. The shipped window is `23:00 → 07:00`, the probes are `warning`, and Session 129 committed at **05:27**. Re-run at 09:37 on the same tree and the same commit the file is seven green, with nothing changed._

_**The contradiction in the symptom was the discriminator.** Silent yet stored-and-adopted looks impossible because `_remember` runs only after a successful send — except in `_adopt`, which writes unconditionally and which DND does not gate. So all three probe titles were adopted by a notifier that had never spoken. The measurement the entry named first, `len(notifier.sent)`, is in the reading now and generalises past its own cause: every other speech reading is a `bool` over a **slice**, so none can tell "the sweep found nothing due" from "a gate above it refused everything", and the total can — driven at `min_severity: critical` with DND off, it fires. **No production change**: the daemon's own comment already says a suppressed reminder does not move the clock, so a fault raised in the window speaks when the window lifts._

_Previously: **One opened and none closed on 2026-08-30 by Session 129, and it was opened by the suite rather than by the work.** `SNAG-TRAY-010`: `tests/test_desktop_store_live.py` fails six of seven here, the premise test among them, and it fails **identically at `62f8e09`, the commit that added it** — so it is not this sitting's regression and not the two before it either. Not residue (zero `sysadmin-live-probe%` rows in `alerts` and `desktop_notifications`) and not the D-Bus transport (`send` is stubbed to a list). Every "did it speak" reading is `False` while the rows are stored and adopted, and the mechanism is deliberately **not** guessed — the entry names the next measurement instead._

_The sitting's own work opened nothing. estate-manager's message `8462bcc5` was answered by [ADR-0006](../adr/0006-wiring-joins-ports.md), admitting `wiring` as the second audit check this repository speaks for; the one limit it found — the producer's `fingerprint` carries no event, so two events on one hook would share one `standing_days` — has an **empty population** (all four hooks declare exactly one event) and belongs to the estate's format rather than to this repository, so it is reported in the message's close note rather than filed._

_Previously: **One closed and one opened on 2026-08-29 by Session 126, and the entry understated its own benefit by two orders of magnitude.** `SNAG-SYSD-005` is **fixed**: `sysadmin-replay-failures.service`, a user unit wanted by `graphical-session.target`, announces at login every unit failure that is still open. The entry priced the loss as the **gap to the next login** — 24 min at best, 6.1 h at worst. The `alerts` table prices it as the **life of the row**, and the one firing that produced a row and was not fixed at once left it open **37.73 hours** (`1 day 13:43:31`, 2026-08-22 18:10:22 → 2026-08-24 07:53:54), of which the login gap is 24 minutes. So what the replay recovers is not lateness, it is **37.3 hours of silence** — because nothing after the login spoke either._

_**The room was not empty for most of it, which is the part the entry had the wrong way round.** Reconstructed from the journal: `start-limit-hit` at 18:11:15, login at 18:34:41, tray started 18:34:46, polled 8500, got nothing and went to `IconState.DISCONNECTED` — where it sat, silently, for **22.2 of the 37.7 hours** across two sessions. Only 15.5 were an empty room. "Four of five fired into an empty room" is true about **firings** and misleading about **silence**: the real fault is that nothing on this box interrupts about a dead daemon, occupied or not, and login is merely the cheapest moment to catch it. That is `SNAG-TRAY-009`, opened here._

_**The blocker the entry deferred on turned out not to be one, and the reason is a property the table already had.** It asked for an `--unannounced` flag at the announcer first, refusing to add one with no reader. That is a **history** predicate; what the replay needs is a **state** one, and `resolve_unit_failures` has exactly one production caller (the lifespan, `main.py:244`) while the `% failed` family sits outside `RESOLVABLE_TITLE_PATTERNS` and retention purges resolved rows only. So an unresolved `systemd_onfailure` row already means *this unit has not come back* — no flag, no announcer change, and the second-speaker trap dissolves with it, since a state predicate cannot speak about a fault that is over._

_**Waiting is permitted here and was refused there, and it is the number that changed rather than the principle.** `SNAG-SYSD-004` measured the gap at 24 minutes against notify-send's 60.08 s window. At login the missing precondition arrives in **seconds**: measured at the 2026-08-23 session, `plasma-plasmashell.service` went active at 14:44:55, the target this unit hangs off was reached at 14:44:57, and plasmashell was still initialising at 14:44:58. `WAIT_BUDGET_SECONDS` is **derived, not chosen** — notify-send's own measured bound, so the replay spends exactly the patience a single blocked call would have, on a mechanism that starts no `plasma_waitforname`. Driven against a private bus: a server claiming the name at t+2 s is caught at **3.02 s** and the notification arrives intact, `urgency=2`, `expire_timeout=0`._

_**Two falsifications passed against deliberately broken code and the second is the one worth carrying.** Eleven mutations each landed red on their intended test. But the live harness's stand-in notification server printed `claimed` and **owned nothing a millisecond later** — `dbus.service.BusName` held only in a local is garbage-collected the moment the function returns — and the wait duly reported `False` after a full budget: a fact about the harness reported as a fact about the module. The first repair was insufficient in the same way, asserting that the stand-in had *said* `claimed`; the mutation satisfied it. The premise now asks the **bus** with `busctl`, which is independent of both the subject and the harness, and the mutation fails naming the harness. Session 125's own trap avoided by construction: mutating the guard to refuse everything turns **three** live tests red and skips none._

_Previously: **One closed and one opened on 2026-08-29 by Session 125, and waiting was measured before it was refused.** `SNAG-SYSD-004` is fixed: the announcer asks whether anything **owns** `org.freedesktop.Notifications` rather than whether the bus socket exists, and against the real script the served bus gives **exit 0 in 31 ms** with the toast on screen while the unserved bus gives **exit 1 in 15 ms** naming the reason — where it previously ran to the 30-second kill. The handoff left "fail fast or wait" genuinely open and it was settled by counting: a pending call **is** delivered (a server claiming the name at t+4 s received it intact, `rc=0`), but the window is notify-send's own **60.08 s** and the next login after the four killed firings was **24 min** away at best and **6.1 h** at worst — **nought of four**, nearest miss **24×** the window. The shipped behaviour was therefore never "wait": `TimeoutStartSec=30` sits under notify-send's 60 s and under the bus's 120 s `service_start_timeout`, so the call could never resolve there at all._

_**The mechanism is one level below what the entry states, and it is what made the wrong question expensive.** A call to an unowned name is not refused — `org.kde.plasma.Notifications.service` declares `Exec=/usr/bin/plasma_waitforname`, so the bus **starts a program whose job is to block until the name appears**, and it outlives the handler systemd kills. `NameHasOwner` is addressed to the bus daemon instead and cannot be caught by it: **five guard calls started zero waiters, one notify-send started one**. Note what was green throughout — `test_the_handler_is_a_oneshot_that_cannot_hang` asserts `TimeoutStartSec` exists and is sane, and 30 seconds is exactly what fired._

_**A falsification passed against deliberately broken code, and the correction the owner made mid-sitting is the more useful one.** Mutating the guard to refuse everything left `test_it_admits_the_live_bus` **skipping** rather than failing, because it asked the guard whether a server existed in order to decide whether to run — a control a broken subject can switch off. And `systemctl reset-failed` returning **exit 0** was written up here as a third correction to this repository's `sudo` claims; it was not. **Polkit prompted the owner on screen and they authorised it**, invisibly to this session, and `pkcheck` says `auth_admin_keep` — admin auth required and *retained*, which is why the confirming retry confirmed nothing. Exit status is evidence about the result, never about the privilege. `SNAG-SYSD-005` carries the residue: the boot-time failure still reaches nobody, and the wait needs an owner that survives a login._

_Previously: **One opened and none closed on 2026-08-29 by Session 124, and the last unchecked entry gained its check.** `SNAG-CFG-003`'s mechanism is exactly as filed — driven at the real `reload_configuration` with the daemon's own injected syncer, a violating config installs with `ok=True` and `jobs_retimed`, sum 25 against a `reminder_hours` of 24. Its **cost** is the smaller half three times: at the *installer*, where the journal's 30 days hold **2 reloads against 122 daemon starts**, so the fix bullet's question about `reload.py` is aimed at the rarest of them and the lifespan's only verdict is about the schema; at the *guard*, where the one inequality it names is **1 of about thirty** live coherence assertions over the two shipped files, all suite-only, one of which returns `SNAG-LOG-003`'s JSON titles when broken; and at the *input*, where lowering the tray's leaf breaks the same ceiling and the reload reports **nothing at all**, because `AppConfig` holds `mute_services` alone under `notifications.tray`. P4 is unmoved — every measurement widens the radius and none finds a population._

_**The check found the trap it was written about, in its own hand and then in the guard.** `load_config` is `set_config(parse_config(...))` — it **installs** — so the drive's helper was writing the value its own witness read back, and a mutation swapping the two changed nothing. `parse_config` was split out for the reload's sake and this is its second caller with the same need. The same trap was live in `tests/test_config_defaults.py`: `_reminder_ceiling` left the process holding `scan_interval_hours: 24`, the configuration that class exists to refuse, for every test behind it — invisible only because its third test happens to reinstall a coherent copy, and `pytest-randomly` makes that a per-seed accident. Both fixed, both pinned._

_**And `SNAG-SYSD-004` is opened**: `sysadmin-failed.service` has been `failed` since 2026-08-23 and **4 of 4** firings since 08-22 were killed at `TimeoutStartSec=30` inside `notify-send`. `Linger=yes` starts `user@1000.service` at boot, so the script's `[[ -S /run/user/1000/bus ]]` guard passes with nobody logged in and the call waits instead of failing. It is silent in the case it exists for — a daemon failing at boot — and `SNAG-DB-005`'s record that this handler *"fired correctly, with a persistent critical toast"* that day is refuted by the journal._

_Previously: **None opened and one closed on 2026-08-29 by Session 121, and this paragraph states no count — `sysadmin-check-snags` derives them and prints them at both ends of every sitting.** `SNAG-DOCS-007` is fixed by the narrowing its own last bullet asked for, one figure short of the wording it used: the **state counts** go, because they have a live second producer, and the **movement** stays, because it has none. _Previously: **One opened and none closed on 2026-08-29 by Session 120** — the paragraph `SNAG-DOCS-007` names, and its figures are struck here rather than left standing, because they were still the register's *current* counts and so still the live second producer the entry is about. Sittings before it keep their numbers on purpose: a paragraph stating what was current at a commit that has passed is frozen, nothing prints it, and rewriting the record of past sittings costs more than a drift it can no longer have._

_**Nothing read this paragraph and the fix is a derivation rather than a check** (the owner's ruling, 2026-08-29). Session 118 found it six sittings stale and Session 119 carried the decision forward; three measurements decided it. It **has no reader** — `claude-preflight.sh` prints `STATUS.md`'s block, both claim reports and the entry bullets, and never this — so `ops_claims` rule 1, which buys that module's legitimacy from *"preflight already prints those claims, with nothing between the document and the reader"*, has no equivalent here. The figure is **derivable**: parsing `git show HEAD~1:docs/roadmap/snag_list.md` through the owning parser gives **100 / 17** against HEAD's **101 / 18**, precisely the movement Session 119 wrote by hand — and a figure a tool can derive should not be a claim a human states, since checking the written count leaves two producers of one fact and deriving it leaves one. The prose is the third reason and the weakest: thirteen paragraphs write that sentence **seven ways**, so a pattern over it would report `unknown` more often than it measured._

_**And the banner it was found beside was stating the open count two ways.** `claude-preflight.sh` counted `^- \[P[0-9]\]` under this heading, which is every bullet whatever its title says: it printed **76 open** eight lines below the snag-claims section's **18 open entries**, with the first ten rows of its list titled **FIXED**. That is `ops_claims` rule 2 — one figure stated two ways — in the surface that sets the agenda, and the wrong half was the one carrying the list. `sysadmin-check-snags --list-open` is the reader now, so `closure_declared` has one implementation rather than a shell approximation free to drift from the number printed above it._

`SNAG-ESTATE-001` came out of **reading** those rows rather than counting
them: two retired PersonalAssistant units have restart-looped 52,178 times
since the 2026-08-04 reorganisation and stalled the kernel on 2026-08-08.
The service-discovery agent had already classified both as `orphaned`, with
the dead path and the reason in plain English, eight days earlier.
`SNAG-DB-002` and `SNAG-SYSD-003` were both found sideways by Session 39 while
looking at something else — a collation warning printed by `psql`, and a retired
`ollama.service` in the unit file being edited. Neither was the session's
subject, and both were recorded rather than fixed for reasons given in the
entries. **`SNAG-DB-002` closed on 2026-08-16 without this repository doing
anything**: its remedy half had been carried out by estate-manager on
2026-08-13, and the three days in which four documents here went on asking for
it are `SNAG-ESTATE-008`.
`SNAG-AGENT-004` was found on 2026-08-11 by looking at the live table to
confirm the Session 39 ladder had fired — 26,270 unresolved alerts for four
retired services, which is the project side's set-based-resolve defect
reappearing on the service side at sixteen times the scale.
`SNAG-PROJ-013` was found by Session 32 on 2026-08-11: the momentum endpoint
cannot see a session in a repository that never dates its handoff.
`SNAG-CFG-001` was found by Session 31, which needed to know which severity is
audible and found that the obvious knob is not the one; the fix turned out to
be a whole dead notification path rather than a stale key. The twelve
`SNAG-PROJ-*` entries from the 2026-08-07 project-organiser capability audit
were **all cleared by [Session 34](tasks.md) on 2026-08-10** and are archived
below with their fix dates. `SNAG-ROADMAP-003` — found 2026-08-08 while writing
the `.project.yaml` manifests — was **fixed on 2026-08-10 (Session 37)** along
with the SessionEnd hook that had been generating the stubs it preferred. The
`BRIEF`/`ROADMAP` entries were
found on 2026-08-07 by the consumer — Alfred now renders `briefing/preview`
daily and is building a page on `/api/projects/board`, so producer-side content
defects have a reader for the first time._

- [P4] SNAG-TEST-002: **the snag-claims exemption rests on a per-check `unknown` discipline that holds 18 of 18 times and that nothing enforces** (2026-08-30, opened by Session 131b as the stated cost of the exemption it took; **FIXED 2026-08-30 by Session 132**)
  - **Symptom**: `tests/test_snag_claims.py` is the one name left in `PRE_CONVENTION` in `tests/test_live_drive_premises.py`. It holds the property that module is about — it reads the live database, transitively through `sysadmin.snag_claims.query_one` — and owes no `@pytest.mark.premise` because its premises are enforced at the **producer**: every way of not-knowing returns `unknown` rather than `match`, which is `schema_guard`'s three verdicts one module over, and the drive asserts those branches by name
  - **Measured 2026-08-30, and the number is why this is P4 and not higher**: **18 of 18** registered checks are driven to an `unknown` verdict by a test inside the class that names them, across **60** such assertions. The discipline is not aspirational, it is total. What is missing is only that it is total *by habit* — `tests/test_snag_claims.py` sweeps `CHECKS` three ways already (every check named by its own entry, no marker naming an unimplemented check, no check naming a closed entry) and none of the three asks whether a check can say it does not know
  - **The population is a nineteenth check, so it is empty today by construction.** That is the same standing as `SNAG-UNITS-006`'s drop-in directories: a mechanism with no members rather than a rule with no reader. A check shipped asserting `match` with no `unknown` branch would report an entry holding hardest on the morning its own reader broke, which is `check_unmarked_sentence_invisible`'s founding argument turned on the family that made it
  - **Why it is filed rather than stated in the docstring.** The blind spot in `_opens_a_live_connection` — it is syntactic, so a drive reaching the database only through a helper dodges the property as well as the glob — **is** stated there, because its remedy is a call graph over `sysadmin/` and Session 131 refused that for its own reasons. This one's remedy is a sweep the size of the three that already exist, which is work somebody can do
  - **Shape of the fix, named so the entry can be closed rather than re-argued**: a fourth sweep over `CHECKS` asserting that each key is reached by at least one test whose body asserts `verdict == "unknown"`, keyed on the class that names the check — the measurement above, promoted from a one-off script into a guard. The witness it needs is its own falsification: driven at a stand-in check with no `unknown` branch it must report that key, or it is `test_the_population_is_real`'s vacuity in a fourth place
  - **What it does not become.** It must not require an `unknown` branch of the *producer* — `check_all` has checks whose every input is local (a file read, an AST walk) and for which not-knowing is genuinely unreachable. The claim is about the **test**, so a check that cannot be driven to `unknown` discharges it by a test saying so, the way `PRE_CONVENTION` discharges rule 2
  - **Check (retired 2026-08-30 with the entry)**: `sysadmin-check-snags` — refuted from **both** ends, which is `check_sysd_ollama_ordering`'s two opposite halves: a sweep over `CHECKS` naming the verdict is the entry's fix landing, and a check that gains no `unknown` drive is the entry's *premise* dying rather than its point being taken. The habit is reported as a figure either way, so a reader can see which happened
  - **Its witness is inside it, and the first draft of that witness was refuted by writing it.** Full coverage is the observation the entry rests on, and a walk that had stopped reading assertions reports it too — so the same walk is driven with a verdict spelling nothing returns, which must reach zero. Spelled as a **literal** that came out wrong: the test asserting it wrote the sentinel into `tests/test_snag_claims.py`, the walk found it, and two checks were reported covered by a verdict that does not exist. `_unwritable_sentinel()` mints one per call, so the zero is by construction rather than by nobody having typed it
  - **The check caught its own author on its first run**, which is the cheapest evidence that it discriminates: written and registered before its tests existed, it reported `unknown_branch_unenforced` as the one check with no `unknown` drive. 18 of 18 at filing, **19 of 19** once its own drive landed
  - **FIXED 2026-08-30 by Session 132. The sweep is the entry's named fix, and the check retired with the entry while its walk did not** — `FROZEN_TABLES`' rule, for the sixth time in this repository. `TestEveryCheckCanSayItDoesNotKnow` in `tests/test_snag_claims.py` sweeps `CHECKS` and refuses a key no class drives to `unknown`; `_unknown_branch_coverage` and `_unwritable_sentinel` moved with it, and `check_unknown_branch_unenforced`, `_sweep_enforces_unknown` and the registration went. **18 of 18** covered at the moment of the fix, the nineteenth being the retiring check itself
  - **The declaration the entry asked for is `UNKNOWABLE`, and it is empty by measurement rather than by omission.** The entry's last bullet forbids requiring an `unknown` branch of the *producer*, so a check whose every input is local discharges the sweep by a name and a stated reason — `PRE_CONVENTION`'s shape one file over, with the same two tripwires: a declared name that has since been driven is reported stale, and one naming no registered check is reported orphaned. A mechanism with no members is `SNAG-UNITS-006`'s standing, so it is driven at a stand-in rather than left to be believed
  - **The looseness of the walk is stated rather than tightened.** It keys on the class, so a class naming a key only in passing while asserting `unknown` about a different one counts — `TestTheQueueTimezoneCheck` names `sysd_ollama_ordering` that way, and `TestChecksAgainstTheLiveBox` covers seven keys at once. Measured: **no key is covered only incidentally**, every one is also reported by the class that owns it. So the sweep is a floor on the habit and not a proof of it, which is the measurement the entry named being promoted rather than a stronger claim being invented in its place
  - **The pin on the sweep's own class fired twice, on its own two drafts**, which is the cheapest evidence it discriminates and is this entry's founding observation arriving inside its own fix. The walk counts a class that names a key *and* asserts the verdict, and this class is one of the classes it walks — so a docstring citing `check_unmarked_sentence_invisible` by key made the sweep vouch for that check, itself. It fired again on the docstring written to explain the first firing, for naming the key while saying that naming it is what is forbidden. `test_live_drive_premises.py` exempts its own owner for exactly this reason; there is no exemption here, so the prose is written around the key
  - **Its witness is inside it, twice over, and the second one was refuted by writing it.** `test_a_verdict_no_test_contains_reaches_nothing` drives the walk at the real file with a minted sentinel, which must reach zero — a walker that had stopped reading assertions reports full coverage exactly as a healthy one does. The second witness asks whether the class pin is green because the class is clean or because the walk is blind *here*: its first draft spliced a key into the class's real source and asserted the walk saw it, and it **failed**, because this class asserts no bare `unknown` constant and therefore could never have been seen. Both halves are supplied now, so the pin's green is a property of the class rather than of the walker
  - **The second guard that passed against broken code was the class's own name, restated.** The pin found its class through a `SWEEP_CLASS` constant sitting beside it — a second statement of a fact the class already carries — so pointing that constant at a *different* clean class left all fourteen tests green: the pin was pinned to whatever the constant said rather than to the sweep. It reads `type(self).__name__` now, which makes the mutation impossible rather than merely caught, and a rename of the class is followed rather than reported. `max_priority_for` against `PRIORITY_MAP`'s rule at the size of a class name
  - **Eleven mutations, each red on exactly the intended test**, two of them wrong on the first attempt and both worth carrying. A comment naming a check key changed nothing, because `ast.unparse` drops comments and the walk unparses too — so the mutation was inert rather than the pin blind, and it had to be respelled as a *docstring*, which `ast.unparse` keeps. And a literal sentinel fails the mint test while leaving the coverage witness green, because a module-level literal sits outside every class the walk reads: the historical defect needed the literal written *into a class*, which is exactly how it was found

- [P3] SNAG-TRAY-010: **`tests/test_desktop_store_live.py` fails six of seven on this box, and it failed identically at the commit that introduced it** (2026-08-30, opened by Session 129; **FIXED 2026-08-30 by Session 130**)
  - **Symptom**: `uv run pytest tests/test_desktop_store_live.py` gives **6 failed, 1 passed**. The premise test is one of the six, so nothing below it means anything — which is exactly the property that test was written to have
  - **Measured, not inferred.** The drive's readings: `spoke_while_watched: False` (correct — the tray gate), and then `spoke_unwatched: False`, `spoke_after_restart: False`, `witness_first: False`, `witness_second: False`, `written_at_announce: False`, `first_count: 0`, `second_count: 0`. **Every "did it speak" reading is False**, so the understudy is not speaking at all rather than speaking wrongly. Yet `stored_titles` and `adopted_titles` both carry all three probe titles, and `inherited_adopted` is `True` where the test expects `False`
  - **Not a regression, and that is the cheap decisive fact.** Driven at `62f8e09`, the commit that *added* the file, on a stashed tree: **6 failed, 1 passed** — the same six. So this sitting did not break it and neither did the two commits since (`ca259e4`, and Session 128's estate work). It has never passed in this environment
  - **Not residue either**, which was the first hypothesis and is refuted: `SELECT` over `sysadmin.alerts` and `sysadmin.desktop_notifications` for `title LIKE 'sysadmin-live-probe%'` returns **zero rows** in both, and the drive's own `pre_existing` reads `set()`. `quieted: 1`, so the drive did reach the live table
  - **Not the notification transport.** `_recorded` replaces `DesktopNotifier.send` with a list that always returns `True`, so this is not `SNAG-SYSD-004`'s D-Bus shape wearing a different hat — and the session bus is live here anyway (`busctl --user status org.freedesktop.Notifications` exits 0, PID 2006582)
  - **What has not been established is the mechanism**, and this entry deliberately stops there rather than guessing one. A snag filed from reasoning about a mechanism instead of from counting its output is the failure `verify-ops-claims-live` names, and this file has recorded that trap twice this month
  - **The next measurement is named.** The drive returns no count of what the notifier *said*, so the first thing to add is `len(notifier.sent)` to the reading — that separates "the sweep never ran" from "it ran and found nothing due". After that, the per-process axis: `pytest-randomly` seeds each run, `_drive` is a module-scoped fixture, and the readings are consistent with a sweep whose clock or presence stand-in is not advancing. Session 115's block claims this was verified live, so the difference is between that process and this one rather than between two revisions
  - **Why P3 and not higher**: it is a test, not the shipped path. `sysadmin/monitor/desktop.py`'s unit tests are green, and the understudy's live population on this box is zero by design — the tray runs, so the tray gate returns before adoption queries. What is lost is the control over `SNAG-TRAY-008`'s fix, which is real: a suite carrying six standing reds trains the next sitting to read a red file as normal, which is how `SNAG-ESTATE-008`'s shape arrives in a test run
  - **FIXED 2026-08-30 by Session 130. The mechanism is the clock, and it is a clock the drive could not see it was reading.** The file supplies **two** leaves and says so in its own docstring — the transport and the two clock readings — and misses a **third**: `DndManager.is_active` calls `datetime.now()` *itself*, so `dnd_manager.should_suppress` reads the real wall clock whatever clock the notifier was handed. The shipped `notifications.dnd.schedule` is **`23:00 → 07:00`** and the probes are raised at `warning`, which `allow_critical: true` does not exempt. Inside that window every send is refused
  - **Which is why the entry could not find it by bisecting.** It is not a property of any revision, so `62f8e09` fails exactly as HEAD does — correctly reported, and it is the *reason* that reading was a dead end rather than evidence. It is a property of **the hour the suite was run**: Session 129 committed at **05:27** and wrote its handoff at **05:23**, both inside the window. Re-run at **09:37** on the same tree and the same commit, the file is **7 passed** with no change to anything
  - **The apparent contradiction was the discriminator all along.** *Silent, yet the rows are stored and adopted* looks impossible because `_remember` runs only after a successful `send` — except in `_adopt`, which writes **unconditionally** (`desktop.py:910`). DND gates `_handle` before the send (`:396`) and the `due` filter before `_restate` (`:602`), and gates adoption **nowhere**. So all three probe titles were adopted and stored by a notifier that had never spoken, and `inherited_adopted: True` is that fact stated a second way
  - **Proved rather than argued, and against the real cause rather than a proxy.** Driven three ways at 09:37 with the tree unchanged: `manual_override=True` gives the six failures verbatim, including which six and which one survives; `manual_override=False` gives seven green; and the **schedule itself** widened to `00:00 → 23:59` — the actual mechanism, not an override standing in for it — also gives the six. The reading dict under DND is `sent_total: 0`, every speech reading `False`, `adopted_titles` 3 of 3
  - **The fix is the third leaf, supplied the way the other two are.** `_hold_dnd_off()` sits beside `standing = understudy.tray_presence` and uses the manager's **public** `set_manual_override`, restoring what it found rather than a literal — the two are different states (`None` defers to the schedule, `False` overrides it) and a drive that walked away leaving it forced-off would silence the window for every test after it in the same process, invisibly, since the effect is a notification nobody receives. Not a narrowing of the subject: gate 1 is supplied one line up for the same reason, and the entry is about the store and adoption, which a gate above them decides nothing about
  - **The two repairs it is not** are named because both are worse. **Skipping** overnight hides a real regression for a third of every day; **failing** overnight is this entry
  - **The measurement the entry named first was the right one, and it generalises past its own cause.** `sent_total` — `len(first.sent) + len(second.sent)` — is now in the reading and asserted non-zero. Every other "did it speak" reading is a `bool` over a *slice* of `sent`, so all of them read `False` whether the sweep ran and found nothing due or a gate above it refused the lot, and the entry's symptom was the whole set reading `False` at once, which no slice can attribute. The total can: a sweep that merely found nothing due still leaves the announce-time sends behind it, so **zero is only reachable above `_handle`**. Driven at `min_severity: critical` with DND off — a *different* gate in the same position — it fires correctly, so it catches the class and not the member
  - **Asserted non-zero and not as a figure**, because the figure is the roll-up's shape and `first_count` / `second_count` already own that. `dnd_suppressing` is recorded beside it and ordered **first**, so the failure names the gate rather than the symptom: unfixed, the loudest line was `spoke_unwatched is False`, a sentence about the sweep for a fault entirely above it
  - **One thing the falsification found that no reading did.** At `min_severity: critical` the drive **errored** rather than failing: `stored[ANNOUNCED_TITLE]` raised `KeyError` while *building* the reading, because `_adopt` admits no rung below the threshold either — so the premise test written to name the cause never ran, and seven errors said nothing. It is `.get`-shaped now. Absent is a reading; a traceback is not, and that is this entry's own lesson arriving inside its fix
  - **No production change, and that was checked rather than assumed.** The daemon's behaviour in the window is correct: a fault raised during DND is not announced, is adopted by the sweep (which anchors its clock), and `desktop.py:598`'s own comment states that a suppressed reminder does not move the clock — so it speaks when the window lifts at 07:00 rather than a full interval later. The defect was entirely in what the harness supplied
  - **Verification**: 3017 → **3018**, +1 and none retired, by stashing to HEAD and re-collecting. Three mutations driven — the leaf unheld (six failures, and the premise now fails first on `dnd_suppressing`), the leaf held but never restored (only the new leak guard red), and a different gate above the sweep (`sent_total` fires). Ruff and mypy clean

- [P4] SNAG-DOCS-007: **the header paragraph still states the movement figure, so two producers of it remain** (2026-08-29, opened by Session 120 as the stated cost of deriving it; **FIXED 2026-08-29 by Session 121**)
  - **Symptom**: `sysadmin-check-snags` now derives `101 entries, 18 open — unmoved since c46872a` and prints it at both ends of every sitting, and the paragraph at the top of this file still writes those figures out in prose. Nothing compares the two. A sitting that mistypes the number leaves a document that disagrees with the box, which is the defect Session 118 found six sittings deep — reduced, not removed
  - **It is the stated cost of the owner's ruling and not a defect in it.** The alternative was a claim-check patterning the figures out of the prose, refused on three measurements: the paragraph has no reader, the figure is derivable, and thirteen paragraphs write the sentence seven ways. What the ruling buys is that the true figure is now printed twice a sitting **beside** the written one, so drift is visible where it was previously invisible; what it does not buy is anything that notices
  - **The blast radius is one sentence and it is bounded by the derivation.** The board publishes from `read_snags` directly and never from this paragraph, so a mistyped figure here reaches no consumer — it misleads the next sitting reading the file and nothing else. That is why this is `P4` and not higher: `SNAG-LOG-014`'s precedent is residue from a fixed mechanism, and this is residue from a fix that landed
  - **A second, narrower limit is filed with it rather than separately**: the movement anchor is a **commit** and not a sitting. Nothing on this box records which commit HEAD was at when preflight ran, and a file recording it would be state free to disagree with the repository. So a checker run *after* this sitting has committed the snag list reports `unmoved` against a subject the reader can see is their own. Both moments the report is actually read — preflight, and postflight before the docs commit — anchor correctly, so the population is a mid-sitting re-run
  - **No check, and the shape of one is named rather than left to be invented.** Session 119 declined a check for `SNAG-CFG-003` on the ground that writing one to this registry's standard is a piece of work the size of a sitting's own, and the same holds here. The shape is `SNAG-ESTATE-012`'s: drive `check_convention` and `check_movement` over two specimens of this document — one with the paragraph's figure altered, one with an open entry removed — and assert the first moves nothing while the second moves the movement line. The second arm is the witness, because an absence is what a broken reader produces for free
  - **It closes by the paragraph dropping the figures**, not by a check being written. A check would measure that the invisibility holds; deleting the numbers and leaving the narrative removes the second producer, which is what the ruling's own reasoning argues for and what a later sitting may decide the narrative can live without
  - **FIXED 2026-08-29 by Session 121, by the narrowing this last bullet asked for — and *“altogether”* was one figure too many.** The paragraph states two things and only one of them has a second producer. The **state counts** (`102 entries, 19 open`) are derived live at every run and printed at both ends of every sitting, so a written copy is `SNAG-DB-003`'s shape and is gone. The **movement** (*“one opened and none closed … the entry is `SNAG-DOCS-007`”*) has no second producer at all, and stays
  - **The measurement that separates them is the anchor's reach, and it is narrower than this entry says.** `MOVEMENT_ANCHOR` is `HEAD` and `measure_movement` compares the **working tree** against it, so the delta is non-zero only between the edit and the commit. Driven at three revisions through the owning parser: `639e594` reads 100 / 17, `c46872a` 101 / 18, `bc43986` 102 / 19 — the series exists in git and the derivation never reports it, because it holds one anchor and no memory of a sitting. It also cannot name **which** entry moved under any anchor, which is the half of the sentence a reader acts on
  - **So this entry's fourth bullet is wrong at one of its two ends, and the sitting's own opening run is what said so.** It reads *“Both moments the report is actually read — preflight, and postflight before the docs commit — anchor correctly, so the population is a mid-sitting re-run”*; preflight runs on a clean tree, where the comparison is `unmoved` **by construction**, and it printed `unmoved since bc43986` at 102 / 19 either side. Postflight before the docs commit is the *only* correct moment, so the population is every other run rather than a re-run. `measure_movement`'s docstring carried the same claim at its owner (*“at the start of a sitting … the delta is exactly what the paragraph wants”*) and is corrected in the same edit — reading the module confirms the claim, running preflight refutes it
  - **Only the named paragraph lost its numbers; the “Previously:” chain keeps its own.** Those state what was current at a commit that has passed, nothing prints them, and they cannot drift — the defect is a *live* figure with a *live* second producer. Session 120's is struck because 102 / 19 was still the register's current count on the day it was closed. Rewriting thirteen historical paragraphs would edit the record of past sittings to remove a drift they can no longer have
  - **No check was written and none is owed, which is the ruling's own reasoning read forward.** The shape this entry named — two specimens, one with the figure altered — is unreachable now, because there is no figure in the paragraph for a specimen to alter. What remains checkable is the derivation itself, and `check_movement` already carries its own red state (the two parsers disagreeing about the open count) plus the nine falsifications Session 120 drove at it

- [P1] SNAG-DB-005: **nothing applies migrations, so `schema_guard`'s refusal is now an outage rather than a warning** (2026-08-24, opened by Session 69; **FIXED 2026-08-24 by Session 70**)
  - **Symptom, measured rather than reported**: `sysadmin.service` was `ActiveState=failed`, `Result=start-limit-hit`, with `ExecMainExitTimestamp=Sun 2026-08-23 08:39:17 BST` — **about 23 hours dead** at the moment it was looked at. The lifespan raised `SchemaRevisionError: database schema is at revision 012 but this code expects 013`, five restarts hit `StartLimitBurst=5`, and systemd stopped trying
  - **The guard did its job exactly.** Refusing to serve against a schema this code was not written for is `SNAG-DB-001`'s whole lesson, and the alternative — coming up degraded — would have written an alert through the schema that was wrong. What failed is the half `SNAG-DB-001` named and did not fix: **nothing applies migrations here.** No `ExecStartPre`, no script, no CI step. `CLAUDE.md` has said so in writing since 2026-08-10
  - **So the failure mode has changed since `SNAG-DB-001` and the entry should say so.** There, an *unapplied* migration let the daemon run and write rows the database rejected — 39 hours of silent blackout. Here the guard converts the same cause into a *loud* 23-hour outage. Loud is the better half of the trade and it is still an outage, because `Restart=always` plus a terminal start limit means the box needs a human either way
  - **The two costs are asymmetric and both were paid.** `service_health` and `filesystem_audits` went unwritten for a day, which the briefing correctly reported as `stale_sources: ['services', 'filesystem']`; and the 03:00 retention purge did not run, so `retention_config.last_purged_at` still reads 2026-08-18 for every table
  - **Found**: 2026-08-24 by Session 69, by restarting the daemon to serve a new route and reading `systemctl show` when it did not come back. Not by the monitor — this service cannot report its own unit being dead, which is what `sysadmin-failed.service` exists for and which did fire (`Triggering OnFailure= dependencies`)
  - **Recovery took two commands and neither needed `sudo`**, which is worth recording because the handoff's note about polkit is about a different verb: `systemctl reset-failed sysadmin` then `systemctl start sysadmin` both returned 0 as `gaddi`. The polkit refusal Session 68 hit was `systemctl kill`
  - **The fix is not "remember to run alembic".** The candidates, in rising cost: an `ExecStartPre=` on the unit (needs a unit edit, so `sudo`); a check in `./scripts/claude-postflight.sh` that a new `alembic/versions/*` file has been applied; or the guard's message becoming an `ExecStartPre` that applies rather than compares — which is the one option that must **not** be taken, because applying a migration unattended at boot is how a bad migration reaches production with nobody watching
  - **FIXED 2026-08-24 by Session 70, and the entry's own ranking of the candidates was wrong in two places.** `ExecStartPre=` was ranked cheapest and buys **nothing**: a check there fails identically to the lifespan guard — same refusal, same `failed`, same 23 hours, one process earlier. It is not a weaker version of the fix, it is the fix already in place, relocated. And postflight alone would **not have caught this outage**: Session 69 restarted the daemon *mid-sitting* to serve a new route, so the box was already down before a session-end check could run
  - **The check runs at the commit, because the commit is the last scripted moment before the restart.** There is no deploy script on this box — the restart is a hand-typed `kill -TERM`. `sysadmin-check-schema` (a console script over `schema_guard.packaged_head()` and a new `live_revision_sync()`) is called by `scripts/check-migrations.sh`, which `claude-precommit.sh` runs **blocking** and `claude-postflight.sh` runs advisory. Driven live: with an unapplied migration present, `git commit` exits 1 and HEAD is unmoved
  - **It blocks whatever is staged, not only a migration file.** The question is the state of the box, not the content of the commit: a database behind the checkout means the daemon is already dead or dies at its next restart, and a docs commit does not make that less true
  - **Exit 2 warns and never blocks, which is the one place this fails _open_ where `schema_guard` fails closed.** A commit refused because PostgreSQL happens to be down teaches the operator to reach for `--no-verify`, which disarms the check for the mismatch it exists to catch — and a commit is not what breaks the box, the restart is. Three verdicts (`match`/`mismatch`/`unknown`) and three exit statuses (0/1/2), because a check that could not look must not report what a check that looked and found nothing wrong reports — `ports_checked`'s rule promoted into a return type
  - **The entry named only prevention, and prevention owns almost none of the 23 hours.** `sysadmin-failed.service` fired *correctly* and its persistent critical toast said only `result=exit-code, exit=1, restarts=5`, pointing at `systemctl status` and `journalctl`. The cause was one revision number and the remedy one command, both of which `schema_guard._REMEDY` had held all along and written **only to the journal** — the surface nobody opens unprompted, which is the same defect this row's own P0 sibling `SNAG-LOG-004` turned on. So `unit_failure._schema_diagnosis()` puts the verdict in the alert row and `notify-unit-failed.sh` puts it in the toast: `monitor/collation.py`'s rule 4 — the remedy's trap is carried in the alert — applied to the fault that needed it most
  - **A healthy schema adds nothing to the message and is still recorded in `details['schema']`**, because "checked, and it was not this" is a different fact from "never checked" and a reader of a stale row cannot tell them apart otherwise
  - **The annotation can never suppress the row it annotates**, and that is the regression this fix could most easily have introduced. `_schema_diagnosis` catches everything and returns `unknown`; a test drives `schema_status` raising and asserts the row is still written and committed
  - **The one duplication this design accepts is the connection, never the rule.** Both new callers run *outside* a running application — a git hook, and a handler that fires when the daemon is dead — so `get_engine()` would raise and a sync reader is unavoidable. What is not duplicated is the schema-qualified table name, the none/one/many interpretation and the wording of a mismatch (`_qualified`, `_interpret_version_rows`, `describe_mismatch`). A live test drives **both** readers against the real `alembic_version` and asserts they agree; falsified by pointing the sync one at `public.alembic_version` — another application's copy, which is exactly what `schema_guard`'s rule 2 exists to prevent — and it fires
  - **Verified live rather than only against fixtures**, and the counterfactual was driven by adding a temporary migration file rather than by stamping the database: that moves the *checkout's* expectation and leaves `alembic_version` untouched, so a crash mid-test cannot leave the box in the state this entry describes. The failure handler was then run for real against the live database with `notify-send` stubbed, giving `CAUSE: …` plus the remedy in both the toast and the row, and **0 rows of residue** after cleanup. The daemon was restarted afterwards and logged `schema_revision_verified: 013`
  - **`sudo systemctl status` was corrected to `systemctl status` in the same lines.** Measured as `gaddi` (wheel): `systemctl status sysadmin` and `journalctl -u sysadmin` both exit 0. A next step the reader can take more easily than they were told is the same defect as the missing remedy, one line up — and it is the second time in two sittings that a `sudo` claim in this repository has been wrong when checked
  - **The one option still not taken is the one the entry rules out by name**: nothing applies a migration. `tests/test_schema_guard.py` asserts the word `upgrade` appears nowhere in `check-migrations.sh`'s executable lines, because that wrapper is the only place a future edit would put it

- [P2] SNAG-LOG-010: **two `noise` recommendations can be word-for-word identical, because the title carries the count and not the signature** (2026-08-24, opened by Session 69; **FIXED 2026-08-24 by Session 72**)
  - **Symptom, from the live endpoint at 07:00 on 2026-08-24**: `GET /api/logs/actions` served two rows both reading exactly `kernel: 39885 occurrences, unchanged`, with the same `occurrences` and the same `severity`. They are two genuinely distinct signatures — the two Bluetooth firmware messages, which sit at equal volume because one kernel retry loop emits both — so the rows are correct and indistinguishable
  - **`_noise_recommendation` builds the title from `source` and `current`**, both of which the pair share. The signature, which is the one field that differs, is in `signature` and in `detail` and not in the title
  - **This is `SNAG-AGENT-005`'s rule unapplied one module over.** That entry moved the signature *into* the alert title precisely because "four open rows all reading `Log error: kernel` are indistinguishable to whoever is looking at the tray". The advice endpoint has the same problem and did not get the same fix
  - **Why it is not urgent**: this is a GET advice surface, so no toast and no row. It costs the reader the ability to tell two recommendations apart, and it makes the pair look like a duplicate — which is worse than a long title, because a reader who assumes it is a duplicate acts on one and leaves the other
  - **The obvious fix has a known trap.** Putting the signature in the title makes it forkable and long — `SNAG-LOG-008`'s rows are ~250 characters of raw JSON — so it wants `SIGNATURE_DETAIL_CHARS` and `truncate_at_word`, the treatment `log_review._quoted_signature` gave the same rows on 2026-08-24
  - **Population is currently zero and will return.** Both kernel signatures fell out of the noise-eligible band on 2026-08-24 (`current` went to 0 as the storm ended, so they classify `GONE`), so the endpoint serves no `noise` rows today. The pair reappears on the next storm
  - **FIXED 2026-08-24 by Session 72 — and both of this entry's own claims about its scope were wrong.** Every title builder in `sysadmin/monitor/log_actions.py` now appends `quoted_signature()`; `capped_signature()` bounds the signature at `SIGNATURE_DETAIL_CHARS` with `truncate_at_word`, and `log_review._quoted_signature` keeps only its `figure_free` gate and borrows the rest, so a review line and an advice title name one signature one way
  - **The family this entry names is the smallest of the three affected.** Driven through the real `recommend()` against the live table at the 2026-08-12 anchor: **14 of 21 rows collided, in five groups** — `New incident on sysadmin.service` ×4, `New incident on kernel` ×3, `New fault from sysadmin.service` ×3, `New fault from sportsanalyser-frontend.service` ×2, and this entry's own `kernel: 39885 occurrences, unchanged` ×2. So "population is currently zero" was true of `noise` and of nothing else: at the live anchor the `noise` population really is zero and **7 of the 9 rows still collided**, every one of them `severity: risk`. The rule went to all four builders rather than to the one that was noticed
  - **Verified on the route rather than on a fixture.** `GET /api/logs/actions` served **7 collisions among 9 rows** at 09:57 and **0 among 9** after the restart at 09:58. The specimen pair was reproduced through the real `build_report` → `recommend()` over the storm window — two rows at 39,885 apiece, now distinct — 18 days before those rows age out, which is what the deadline was for
  - **Two things the fix corrected in what was already written down.** The word `unchanged` was asserted in the title for all four change kinds `_is_noise_candidate` admits, and both live rows classify **`FALLING` — 39,885 this window against 77,496 last** — so the title claimed flatness about a signature that had halved while the row's own `detail` printed the two numbers contradicting it. It is gone; the count stays, because volume is the reason to act. And **12 member signatures were being sliced mid-word with no marker**, one ending `"message": "alert_raised", "service"` — the unmarked cut that `log_review._quoted_signature`'s docstring calls `SNAG-BRIEF-002`, and calls *worse* here, in the module that lent it the constant
  - **The cost is stated rather than hidden**: `sysadmin.service`'s signatures are whole JSON records, so four of the nine live titles now open with `{"timestamp": "N-N-N …`. That is `SNAG-LOG-008` becoming visible, and it is the trade `alert_title` already made and `SNAG-LOG-003` already paid for — an ugly title a reader can tell apart beats a tidy one they cannot. What the cap leaves behind is `SNAG-LOG-013`
  - **The tests were what hid it.** Every title in the module was pinned by exactly one assertion — `rows[0].title.startswith("New fault from")` — which the defect passes intact, `TestJournalCommand` one sitting over. All nine new tests were falsified against the behaviour they replace, and one had to be strengthened before it could be: it compared the two modules' quoting on a *short* signature, where a slice and a marked cut agree, so it passed against the broken code

- [P3] SNAG-TEST-001: **three guards in `test_snag_claims.py` failed once and have not failed since, and nothing explains it** (2026-08-26, opened by Session 91, **fixed and closed 2026-08-26** by Session 92)
  - **Symptom, observed once**: a run of `uv run pytest tests/test_snag_claims.py` reported `3 failed, 104 passed`, naming `TestChecksAgainstTheLiveBox::test_a_disambiguated_title_refutes_the_headline_half_alone` and `::test_a_sibling_aware_roll_up_refutes_the_detail_half_alone` — both `SNAG-LOG-013`'s check — and a third the output cut off. The run immediately before it was a clean full suite and the only edit between them was a **docstring** in an unrelated test class
  - **Not reproduced in 15 runs since**: 5 whole-file runs and 10 targeted runs, all green, plus a clean full suite either side
  - **What has been ruled out, by reading the path rather than by assuming**: the probe is **pure**. `check_capped_signature_collides` builds its own `LogTrendReport` against a fixed `anchor = 2026-08-25 12:00 UTC`, and `log_actions.recommend` takes `declared_noise`, `scopes` and `related` as arguments and reads none of them itself — so there is no clock, no database and no unit file anywhere in what those two tests drive. And there is **no randomising plugin** installed (pytest 9.0.2, no `pytest-randomly`, no `xdist`), so collection order is stable run to run, which is what would otherwise explain a rare inter-test leak
  - **So the two obvious causes are both excluded and no third is offered.** That is the entry: a guard that fails once in twenty runs is worth less than its green runs suggest, and the honest state is that nobody knows why — which is a claim to record rather than a diagnosis to publish
  - **Cost is bounded and it is the guard's, not the box's**: `SNAG-LOG-013`'s check is a documentation instrument, so a spurious red costs a sitting's confidence in the sweep rather than anything the daemon serves. It is filed at P3 for that reason and not because the non-determinism is acceptable
  - **Where to start**: run the file under `-p no:cacheprovider` in a loop until it fires, then `--lf -vv` for the third name the tail dropped. The third failure is the missing evidence — two of the three share a check and the third may not
  - **Found**: 2026-08-26 by Session 91, while re-measuring the entry counts for an unrelated check
  - **FIXED 2026-08-26 — the cause is `hash()`, and it sits upstream of the path the entry read.** All three guards patch in a hypothetical fix whose only job is to render two signatures that agree past the cap *apart*, and all three built that disambiguator as `` hash(s) % 997 ``. CPython seeds `str` hashing from `PYTHONHASHSEED`, so the value is stable inside a process and different between them: when the probe pair happens to collide mod 997, the stand-in disambiguates nothing, `check_capped_signature_collides` correctly reports the entry **unrefuted**, and all three guards fail together having measured the stand-in's luck rather than the check's sensitivity
  - **Both exclusions in the entry above are correct, and neither could have reached it.** The probe *is* pure — no clock, no database, no unit file — and there *is* no randomising plugin, so collection order *is* stable. The secret `hash` reads is established before the interpreter imports anything, so it is upstream of the code path being read: no amount of reading that path finds it. That is the transferable half. **A defect whose input is the process cannot be excluded by reading the process's code**
  - **The third name is recoverable from the population, and the population is closed.** Builtin `hash(` occurs **exactly three times in the repository**, at `tests/test_snag_claims.py:618`, `:637` and `:657`, all three inside `TestChecksAgainstTheLiveBox` — so the third guard the tail dropped is `test_a_divergence_aware_cap_refutes_both_halves`, adjacent to the two the entry names. Reproduced deterministically at `PYTHONHASHSEED=282`: **`3 failed, 104 passed`** and those three names, which is the entry's own symptom to the digit
  - **The rate is 1 in 997, not 1 in 20, and the entry's own method could not have found it.** Measured rather than reasoned about: **19 collisions over 20,000 hash seeds** — 1 in 1,053, indistinguishable from the modulus. The recommended loop was run as the control — **40 whole-file runs under `-p no:cacheprovider`, all green**, which is exactly what 1/997 predicts. Even odds need ~690 runs and 99 % confidence ~4,600, which is over seven hours at 6 s a run; the seed scan hit its first collision at 282 and took under a minute. **A per-process defect is hunted per process, not per run** — the loop varies the wrong axis, because the thing that differs between two runs is the one input the loop cannot see
  - **That corrects the entry's cost bullet in the direction that matters.** It reads *"a guard that fails once in twenty runs"*; at once in 997 the nuisance is 50x smaller and the diagnostic is 50x worse — the fifteen green runs were never evidence of anything, and neither would the next nine hundred have been. A guard's green run is worth what its failure rate says it is worth, and nobody had measured the rate
  - **The fix is the same short marker with the randomisation removed.** `_marker()` is `blake2s(text.encode(), digest_size=4).hexdigest()`, so whether the probe pair separates is a property of the text alone — a fact this file **asserts** (`TestTheStandInDisambiguator::test_it_separates_the_probe_pair`) rather than a probability it lives with. `ports_checked`'s rule at the instrument rather than at the report
  - **The counter-intuitive result, and it is why the AST sweep shipped.** Driven at the old behaviour in both directions: at seed 282 all six guards fire, and at seed 0 **the three original guards are green** and only the source sweep and the purity pin fire. So `test_it_separates_the_probe_pair` inherits the very 1/997 it exists to remove and is red at 1 run in 997; `test_no_guard_here_reaches_for_the_randomised_builtin` — which merely refuses the builtin, by AST so the docstrings full of the word fall out — is red at **every** seed. **Banning the instrument beats measuring the property**, which is the reverse of this registry's usual ordering and holds only because the property is the thing that is randomised
  - **No registry check, and the absence is principled rather than owed.** The claim is about this suite's own instrument, so a `sysadmin-check-snags` entry would read its own output — `SNAG-ESTATE-014`'s reason for having none. The guard is a test because the thing guarded is a test. So `convention:unchecked` falls **13 → 12** by the entry leaving the open set, and `checked` holds at **12**, which is Session 89's shape again: a sitting that closed something reads identically to one that checked nothing unless both counts are read together

- [P3] SNAG-ESTATE-014: **eighteen of the twenty-six open entries carry no check, so their claims are only as fresh as the last hand sweep** (2026-08-25, opened by Session 84, **closed 2026-08-27** by Session 103)
  - **Symptom**: `sysadmin-check-snags` re-measures the claim of **8** open entries. The other **18** are reported by name and counted, and nothing else looks at them between hand sweeps. Session 82's sweep cost a whole sitting and found five entries dead on the box, three of them `P1` and three fixed for between nine and thirteen days; the same sweep is owed again the moment anything lands
  - **Not a gap in the machinery — it is the machinery's own report.** `check_convention`'s `convention:unchecked` finding is what publishes the sixteen, so this entry is measured on every run by the thing it is about. That is deliberate: `SNAG-ESTATE-012` records that a claim with no pattern and no marker is invisible, and the fix for invisibility is to be counted, not to be checked
  - **Why the eighteen were not checked here**: four are **delegated** (`SNAG-ESTATE-002`, `-004`, `-006`, `-007`, plus `SNAG-LOG-012`) and their claims are about another repository's code, so a check would be a cross-repo read of a thing that repository is already fixing; several more are **judgements rather than measurements** (`SNAG-SVC-001`'s conflict with `known_noise` rule 3, `SNAG-UNITS-003`'s trade, `SNAG-ESTATE-012` itself), and a check that scored a judgement would be inventing a number the entry deliberately does not have
  - **The delegated half of that reason is wrong, and twice over** (corrected 2026-08-26 by Session 91). Two of the five it names now carry checks. `SNAG-ESTATE-002`'s (Session 88, the eleventh) broke it once and the note conceded the ground narrowly — a check is worth writing *because* the entry is delegated, since the closing move happens in a tree nothing here watches. `SNAG-LOG-012`'s (the fourteenth) breaks the stated reason itself: it costs **no cross-repo read at all**, because `estate-lib` is an editable install and their fix arrives through this repository's own import. So "a check would be a cross-repo read" was never a property of delegation; it was a property of the two instruments that happened to be written first. The judgement half stands unamended
  - **Shape of a fix**: one check per sitting, not eighteen in one. Each is a claim to be re-derived and a falsification to be driven, and the eight built here took a sitting between them. The honest order is by what a wrong answer costs: an entry whose claim is about *this* box first, a delegated one last
  - **Why P3**: it is the residue of a fix rather than a defect, and it shrinks by one every time someone writes a check. It rises if the count stops moving for several sittings, because a convention nobody extends is one that will be read as complete
  - **Check**: none, and the absence is the entry. Its claim *is* the count `convention:unchecked` publishes, so a check for it would read its own output. Written first as `<!--check:none_yet-->` and refused by the machinery within a minute — a marker names a check, and "there is no check" is not one; the report said so as an unimplemented key, which is the convention catching its author using it to state an absence
  - **18 → 17 on 2026-08-25 (Session 85)**, which is the shape-of-fix bullet above being followed rather than a correction to it: `SNAG-LOG-013` now names `capped_signature_collides`. The title's figure is left as filed — it records what was true when the entry was opened, and the live count is `convention:unchecked`'s to publish, which is this entry's whole argument. One inconsistency worth naming while here: the second bullet says the finding publishes "the sixteen" where the symptom and the title say eighteen, so the entry states its own figure two ways — `ops_claims.py` rule 2's drift, inside the entry about being unchecked
  - **17 → 14 across Sessions 86, 87 and 88** (2026-08-26), which is the shape-of-fix bullet being followed at one check a sitting: `SNAG-ROADMAP-001` (checked, then **closed**, its check leaving the registry with it), `SNAG-ESTATE-002` and now `SNAG-DOCS-005`. Two of those three were **delegated**, against this entry's own ordering, which put a delegated claim last — and the reason is the one that ordering missed: being delegated is what makes a check worth having, since the closing move happens in a tree nothing here watches. `SNAG-DOCS-005` is the counter-case that keeps the ordering honest, a claim about this repository's own code that was checkable in a sitting
  - **14 held on 2026-08-26 (Session 89), and it is the first sitting where the count stayed put for the interesting reason.** `SNAG-DOCS-005` was refuted by its own check and closed, so a checked entry left the open set and the registry at once: open 25 → 24, checked 11 → 10, unchecked 14 → 14. That is the shape-of-fix bullet reaching its end state rather than stalling — the ordering above says which entry to check next and says nothing about what happens when a check succeeds. What happens is that this number does not move, which reads identically to a sitting that checked nothing. Only the *checked* count tells the two apart, so read them together
  - **13 → 12 on 2026-08-26 (Session 92), and it moved without a check being written.** `SNAG-TEST-001` was closed, so an *unchecked* entry left the open set: open 25 → 24, checked **12 → 12**, unchecked **13 → 12**. That is the third way this number moves and the entry had named only two — a check written (the shape-of-fix bullet) and a checked entry closing (Session 89's bullet, where it does not move at all). An unchecked entry closing moves it for the same reason a check does and buys nothing: nobody is measuring anything new, the denominator simply shrank. So the pair of counts still does not separate the three cases, and the ordering bullet above is the only thing that says which sitting did work worth having
  - **The one it closed is also the counter-example to this entry's population.** `SNAG-TEST-001` never carried a check and never should have: its claim is about *this suite's own instrument*, so a `sysadmin-check-snags` entry would read its own output. It sat in `convention:unchecked` for a day as though it were owed one. The finding counts entries with no check and cannot tell “not yet” from “never” — `ports_checked`'s rule at the level of the register rather than the reading
  - **Found**: 2026-08-25 by Session 84, as the stated starting position of the machinery rather than by observation
  - **May it declare itself *unmeasurable by rule*? No — judged 2026-08-27 by Session 102, and the refusal costs it nothing because it already has what the declaration would buy.** Its claim *is* the count `convention:unchecked` publishes, and that finding **names it every sitting**. Being counted is what its own second bullet argues is the fix for invisibility, so an exemption would move it from a published bucket to no bucket in exchange for a property it already holds
  - **And it would be the first user of the category to subtract itself from its own subject.** The entry's claim is "N open entries carry no check"; it is one of the N. Exempting it makes the sentence true by editing the sentence's subject, which is `ops_claims` rule 6's shape — nothing there writes to a document, because a check that corrects the file it reads becomes a second author of the claim. The count would also gain a **fourth** way to move, on top of the three this entry has already catalogued (a check written, a checked entry closing, an unchecked entry closing), and the entry's own bullets record that the pair of counts cannot separate the first three. A fourth makes the number less legible, not more
  - **The machinery had already refused the weaker form of this**, which is the precedent rather than an argument: `<!--check:none_yet-->` was written first and rejected within a minute, because a marker names a check and "there is no check" is not one. A `covered-by` bullet for this entry is the same move with a longer name
  - **What is legitimate here is the state it is already in**, and the judgement leaves it there: named by the finding it is about, with no marker, and with the reason stated in the body so a reader does not take the absence for an oversight — `SNAG-ESTATE-012`'s treatment of a sentence no pattern can reach, applied to an entry no check can measure
  - **3 → 2 on 2026-08-27 (Session 102)**: `SNAG-SVC-001` now names `check_interval_looks_away` — open unmoved at **24**, checked **21 → 22**, unchecked **3 → 2** (measured either side of the edit by driving `load_entries`, and by estate-manager's `read_snags`, which reads **93 rows / 24 open** both times). Two open entries carry no check: this one and `SNAG-ESTATE-006`, whose judgement above rules it a live candidate rather than an exempt one. So the shape-of-fix bullet has **one** entry left to follow and then nothing, which is the first sitting at which this entry can see its own end
  - **The series above stopped at Session 92 and the figures in it are nine sittings stale** — it reads 12 unchecked where the box reads 2, because Sessions 93–101 each wrote a check and none of them came back to the prose. That is not an oversight to correct bullet by bullet; it is this entry's own argument arriving at its own body. Its second bullet says the live count is `convention:unchecked`'s to publish, so a running total kept in prose beside it is a second statement of one fact that can disagree — `ops_claims` rule 1's shape, and the drift is now the *fourth* thing this entry states two ways. The series is left as filed for the reason the title's figure is: it records what was true when each line was written
  - **Closed 2026-08-27 by Session 103, and neither of the two triggers it was handed is the reason.** `SNAG-ESTATE-006` now names `audit_code_unpublished`, which was the last entry the shape-of-fix bullet had to follow: open unmoved at **24**, checked **22 → 23**, unchecked **2 → 1** (measured either side of the edit by driving `load_entries`, and by estate-manager's `read_snags`, which reads **93 rows / 24 open** both times). The sitting was asked whether `convention:unchecked` **reaching zero** should retire this entry, and the measurement refutes the premise: it reaches **one**, and the one is this entry. It cannot reach zero while this entry is open, because the judgement two bullets up rules it permanently unmeasurable — so *reaching zero* was never a trigger anything could produce, and a sitting waiting for it would have waited for ever
  - **What closes it is that the entry is about a harm and not about a count.** Its own sentence is *“so their claims are only as fresh as the last hand sweep”*, and the number of entries in that condition is now **zero**: every open entry but this one carries a check, and this one's claim is published on every run by the finding it is about, which its second bullet argues is the fix for invisibility. The count is one and the harm is nought, and the entry is about the harm. That reading is what makes this a completion rather than a definitional trick, and it is available without touching the count at all
  - **The objection this entry raised against exempting itself is faced rather than dodged, because it does reach the closure.** *“It would be the first user of the category to subtract itself from its own subject”* is true of a closure as much as of an exemption — the effect on the sentence is identical, the count goes 1 → 0 because the entry left. What separates them is not the effect but whether the work is finished. An exemption leaves the entry live and edits what the count counts; a closure says there is nothing left to do, and the shape-of-fix bullet's *one check per sitting* has run out of entries. So the refusal recorded there stands unamended and does not extend to this
  - **The machinery survives the closure and loses nothing, which was read rather than assumed.** `check_convention` computes `unchecked` from the live entries and the registry, so it needs no entry to hold it open: a future open entry with no check is named by the finding whether or not anything is filed about the convention. **Demonstrated within the sitting and by accident** — `SNAG-DOCS-006` was filed in the same edit, so the report went straight back to `1 of 24` naming it, with this entry closed. That is the argument for closing this one, arriving as evidence instead of as reasoning
  - **The cost is a fifth way for the count to move that this entry did not catalogue: it stops existing.** `check_convention` appends the finding only when the unchecked set is non-empty, so a report in which every open entry is checked carries **no line about it at all** — and a reader cannot tell that from the finding having been deleted. `ports_checked`'s rule arriving at the register, and the three ways this entry catalogued become five (a check written, a checked entry closing, an unchecked entry closing, this entry closing, and the finding going silent at zero). Filed as `SNAG-DOCS-006` rather than fixed here, because the fix needs a verdict for a convention finding that holds — `_convention` returns `unknown` unconditionally, so emitting a zero line today would pin `sysadmin-check-snags` at exit 2 for ever, which is a change to the exit-status contract `claude-precommit.sh` reads and not a line to add in passing
  - **Not filed as `SNAG-ESTATE-015`, and the reason arrived this morning.** estate-manager's message `153c1c96` records that the `SNAG-ESTATE-*` namespace has **two minters and no owner**: all 14 ids this document defines collide with one of their 71, every colliding pair names a different defect, and an id-shaped lookup therefore answers *plausibly and wrongly* rather than not at all. A fifteenth id minted into it this afternoon would be the fifteenth collision. `SNAG-DOCS-*` is this repository's alone — measured, they mint none — and it is the more accurate area anyway, since neither that entry nor this one is about the estate: both are about this repository's own claims machinery, which is what `SNAG-ESTATE-014` should have been called on the day it was opened

- [P3] SNAG-DOCS-006: **`convention:unchecked` is emitted only when the set is non-empty, so a register in which every open entry is checked says nothing rather than saying nought** (2026-08-27, opened by Session 103 as the stated cost of closing `SNAG-ESTATE-014`, **FIXED 2026-08-28** by Session 113)

  - **Symptom**: `check_convention` appends the `convention:unchecked` finding inside `if unchecked:`, so a report where the unchecked set is empty carries no line about it at all. A reader of `sysadmin-check-snags` cannot tell *every open entry carries a check* from *that finding was removed, renamed or is failing to run* — the two look identical, and only one of them is good news
  - **Cause**: the finding was written when the set could not plausibly be empty — `SNAG-ESTATE-014` opened it at **18 of 26** — so the zero case was never a state anyone had to render. It became reachable for the first time on 2026-08-27, when the twenty-fifth check took the set to one and that one was `SNAG-ESTATE-014` itself
  - **This is `ports_checked`'s rule arriving at the register.** That field exists because a sweep whose `ss` call failed reports zero findings and zero-because-blind must not be served as zero-because-clean; `jobs_synced` is the same rule one domain over, and `truncated_fraction`, `unread_surfaces` and `skipped_checks` are it again. The register is the one surface where the rule was never applied, because nobody had seen the zero
  - **Population is empty today, and it emptied by this sitting's own hand**: closing `SNAG-ESTATE-014` took the set to nought and filing *this* entry put it straight back to one, so the report reads `1 of 24 … SNAG-DOCS-006`. Stated rather than left to be discovered, because *“population is zero”* is what mis-ranked `SNAG-ESTATE-006` for eleven days, and because the emptiness here is a fact about the afternoon rather than about the defect
  - **Not fixed in the sitting that found it, and the reason is a contract rather than effort.** `_convention` returns `unknown` unconditionally — every convention finding is a fault by construction — so emitting a zero line today would pin `sysadmin-check-snags` at exit **2** for ever. `claude-precommit.sh` and `claude-postflight.sh` read that status, and `schema_guard.EXIT_STATUS` gives `unknown` its own rung precisely so a check that could not run is told apart from one that failed. The fix therefore needs a *verdict* for a convention finding that holds, which is a change to what the three convention families mean and not a line to add in passing
  - **Two shapes were considered and one is a trap.** Emitting the line only when the set is empty is the cheap version and rebuilds the defect mirrored: a reader then cannot tell a *non*-empty report from a missing finding. Whatever lands must publish the same line in both states, which is what `ports_checked` does
  - **Check**: none yet. Its claim is about this module's own report, so a check would read its own output — `SNAG-ESTATE-014`'s reason, inherited along with the defect. Unlike that entry it is *not* permanently unmeasurable: the report is a list of `Finding` objects a test can drive `check_convention` for, which is how the fix will be pinned when it lands
