  - **Why not `SNAG-ESTATE-015`**: estate-manager's message `153c1c96` (2026-08-27) records that the `SNAG-ESTATE-*` namespace has two minters and no owner — all 14 ids this document defines collide with one of their 71, every pair naming a different defect, so an id-shaped lookup answers plausibly and wrongly. `SNAG-DOCS-*` is this repository's alone, measured against their list the same afternoon, and it is the more accurate area besides: this is the snag machinery, not the estate
  - **Found**: 2026-08-27 by Session 103, by closing the entry whose population it was the last member of, rather than by observation
  - **Fixed 2026-08-28 by Session 113.** `check_convention` appends the finding in all three states and `_convention` takes a `verdict`, defaulting to `unknown` so the other two families are untouched. A non-empty set is `unknown` and unchanged; an empty set **over a population** is `match`; an empty set over **no open entries at all** is `unknown` again. Live either side: `?? … 1 of 21 … SNAG-DOCS-006` became `ok … 0 of 20 open entries carry no check`, and the report's exit went **2 → 0** — the first time `sysadmin-check-snags` has exited 0 since it was written, because this entry was the last unchecked one and the only `??` in a report of 20 holding claims
  - **The exit-status contract the entry was filed on is honoured rather than changed.** It refused the fix in the sitting that found it because `_convention` returned `unknown` unconditionally, so a zero line would pin the tool at exit **2** for ever. Giving *one* family a verdict is what avoids that: `marker:` and `pin:` are faults by construction — there is no state of the world in which either line is good news — so they keep the default, and `scripts/check-snag-claims.sh`'s documented statuses need no edit, since an unchecked entry still reports `unknown` and still exits 2
  - **The third state is the one the fix could most easily have got wrong, and it is `ports_checked`'s rule one level inside its own application.** A document whose entries are *all closed* parses cleanly — `load_entries` reports a problem only when it reads no entries at all — so it reaches the count with `open_total == 0` and a nought nothing could have made non-zero. Served as `match` that is zero-because-blind wearing zero-because-clean, which is the defect being fixed; it is `unknown`, and the note says the population is absent rather than clean. Reachable and empty today
  - **Two of the five falsifications passed against deliberately broken code**, which is the part worth carrying. `overall([])` is `match` and exits `0`, so the exit-status test written as two status assertions *agreed with the silence it exists to catch*; and the vacuous fixture carried no marker, so `pin:fake_one` fired and supplied the `2` the branch under test was meant to supply — a test green for a reason unrelated to its subject. Asserting the finding's **presence** before its status is what makes it discriminate. Session 78's `waived=True` test and Session 80's interned-string check, met a third time
  - **One of the five passes against the old code and says so.** `test_an_unchecked_entry_is_still_unknown` is a regression pin — the fix must not have widened what an *unchecked* entry reports — and a pin that could fail against the behaviour it preserves would be pinning the wrong thing
  - **No check is added, and its absence is now the finding's own subject.** The entry's `Check:` bullet said a test could drive `check_convention` for its `Finding` objects, which is what `TestTheUncheckedLineIsPublishedInEveryState` does; a registry entry would additionally have to name an open entry, and this one is closing. `SNAG-ESTATE-014`'s closure took its check with it; this one never had one to take, so checks-in-registry is unmoved at **20**

- [P3] SNAG-DOCS-005: **`ops_claims` would read a quoted marker as a real one, and the document most likely to quote one is the document it does not read** (2026-08-25, opened by Session 84, **fixed and closed 2026-08-26** by Session 89)
  - **Symptom**: `ops_claims.read_markers` matches `<!--check:key-->` over the flattened printed region with no regard for markdown code spans, so a sentence in `STATUS.md` that *discusses* the convention — ``the `<!--check:routes-->` marker`` — would register as a claim that the block names `routes`. The failure is quiet in both directions: a quoted key nothing implements is reported as a broken marker, and a quoted key that *is* implemented silences the "unclaimed figure" finding for a sentence that claims nothing
  - **Measured empty, and measured rather than assumed**: `STATUS.md`'s printed region carries **9** markers on 2026-08-25 and **none** of them is inside a code span. The population is empty because the block is written to be read, not to teach the convention
  - **Found by the sibling hitting it with a full population.** `snag_claims` shipped with `MARKER_RE` copied in shape from `ops_claims`, and its first live run reported two checks nobody implements — `helth` and `routes` — both "named by `SNAG-ESTATE-011`", an entry that names neither and merely quotes them as examples, one of them deliberately misspelled. `snag_list.md` is the one document on this box that writes *about* markers, so it is where the hazard has members
  - **Fixed on one side only, deliberately**: `snag_claims.strip_code_spans` removes code spans before markers are read, and its docstring names this entry. Sharing the helper would mean one composition root importing another to borrow a regex, which is a coupling worth more than the copy — so the two are pinned by this entry rather than by an import
  - **Shape of a fix**: three lines in `ops_claims.read_markers`, plus a test with a backticked marker in the fixture region. The cost of leaving it is one sitting's confusion the first time `STATUS.md` explains its own convention to a reader
  - **Why P3**: empty population today, and the failure is a confusing report rather than a wrong measurement of the box
  - **Found**: 2026-08-25 by Session 84, by running the new sibling rather than by reading the old one
  - **Check (`quoted_marker_reads_as_real`, removed 2026-08-26 with the entry)**: `sysadmin-check-snags` — refuted when a quoted marker stops registering as a real one. **Reproduced, never counted**: the mechanism is driven against a `STATUS.md` this check builds, in the three shapes the defect is quiet in — a quoted key nothing implements *inventing* a broken-marker finding, a quoted key that is implemented *silencing* the unclaimed-figure finding beside it (run against a control region first, since a finding's absence is only evidence once its presence has been observed), and the same quotation inside a **doubled fence**, which is where the two obvious three-line fixes part company — a code-span pattern closing on a same-length backtick run removes the marker and `` `[^`]+` `` closes at the inner backtick and leaves it bare. This entry's own body carries that shape
  - **The empty population is being *paid for*, not enjoyed, and the margin is nine lines** (measured 2026-08-26 by the check above, refining this entry's second bullet). That bullet reads the emptiness as a property of how the block is written — *"written to be read, not to teach the convention"* — and the block itself says otherwise in its own prose: *"One thing this block deliberately does not do: quote a marker"*, naming this entry and sending the reader to `sysadmin/ops_claims.py` *"where quoting it is safe"*. So the cost this entry files as a future sitting's confusion is **already being paid**, as a sentence the dashboard cannot write. Beyond the block the region runs to 270 lines and its far end is empty only by placement: the nearest quoted marker sat **9 lines** past the region's last line when this was measured, naming `handoff_apology_published` — a check retired with `SNAG-ROADMAP-001` that morning, so the *inventing* direction has a live member — and the sentence you are reading pushed it further out within the hour, which is why the figure is recorded as history rather than restated as a claim. The check prints the live distance on every run
  - **Closed 2026-08-26 by Session 89, on a run rather than on the argument for it.** `sysadmin/ops_claims.py` gained `CODE_SPAN_RE` — ``(`+)[\s\S]*?\1``, closing on a backtick run of its **own length** — and `read_markers` strips code spans before matching, which is the three-line fix this entry's own bullet asked for. The check reported **refuted** on the same run, with all three probe lines reading `False` where they had read `True` twenty minutes earlier: the quoted implemented key no longer silences the `unclaimed` finding beside it, the quoted unimplemented key no longer invents a broken-marker one, and the doubled fence no longer leaks
  - **The check paid for itself by refusing the fix a reader would have written first.** Driven at the naive ``` `[^`]+` ``` the report came back **`match`** — *"the single fence is handled and the doubled fence still leaks … what remains is a narrowing rather than a closure"* — and driven at the same-length pattern **`mismatch`**. Both were run before the entry was touched. That is the third probe existing for exactly the reason it was written down for, and it is the difference between an entry closed on a measurement and one closed on a plausible-looking diff
  - **The check left the registry with the entry**, which `tests/test_snag_claims.py::test_every_checked_entry_is_open` makes a rule rather than a choice — a refuted check on a closed entry says "go and judge this" for ever. `ops_probe`, `survey_quoted_markers`, `MarkerSurvey`, `QuotedMarker`, `_quoted_only` and the six `OPS_PROBE_*` constants went with it, as `handoff_apology_published`'s helpers did one sitting earlier. **What did not go is the pin**: `tests/test_ops_claims.py::TestAQuotedMarkerIsAQuotation` drives the two modules' code-span patterns over seven shapes and asserts they agree character for character, so the copy `snag_claims.strip_code_spans` deliberately is cannot drift now that no check watches it — import where you can, pin where you cannot
  - **The last measurement the survey ever took, recorded because the instrument is now deleted** (2026-08-26): `STATUS.md`'s printed region carried **9** markers with **0** quoted, and **4** quoted outside it, the nearest **76** lines past the region's end naming `handoff_apology_published` — the retired check, so the *inventing* direction still had a live member on the day the fix landed. The margin was 9 lines when the previous bullet was written and 76 when this one was; the number never stopped moving, which is why the emptiness was never a property of the document
  - **The sentence the block could not write, it now writes.** The cost this entry filed as a future sitting's confusion was being paid as *"One thing this block deliberately does not do: quote a marker"*; `STATUS.md` now quotes one instead, inside a code span, as the live demonstration that the fix holds. A regression in `read_markers` makes that sentence produce a spurious `marker:` finding at the top of the next sitting, which is the loud direction

- [P3] SNAG-LOG-013: **capping a signature can put two rows back where `SNAG-LOG-010` found them, and inside one live incident row it already has** (2026-08-24, opened by Session 72)
  - **Symptom, measured on the live report at both anchors**: **9 of 55 signatures share their first `SIGNATURE_DETAIL_CHARS` characters**. All nine are `sysadmin.service`'s raw JSON records, which agree as far as `"logger": "sysadmin.core.agent", "message": "alert_raised` and diverge only past the cap
  - **No *title* collides today**, and the reason is not this module's doing: those nine are swallowed as members of one incident roll-up, so only the anchor's signature ever reaches a title. `group_incidents` is what is holding it, and it holds by accident
  - **One level down it is already visible.** The live row headed `New incident on sysadmin.service — "{"timestamp": …` lists **7 members that are word-for-word identical after capping**, and a second row lists 2. The roll-up's promise is that it *names* every signature it swallows (`SNAG-ESTATE-001`, which cost a session); a name shared by seven members names none of them
  - **The cap is not the cause and raising it is not the fix.** These signatures are unreadable because this box's one JSON-writing source puts a whole record on a line (`SNAG-LOG-008`); any bound is defeated by two records that differ past it, and a larger number only moves where
  - **Two candidate fixes, neither cheap**: make the signature readable at the producer, which is `SNAG-LOG-008`'s own fix and removes the class; or cap from the first character at which the group's members diverge, which needs the sibling set and so cannot live in a per-row pure function the way `capped_signature` does
  - **Cost is bounded**: a GET advice surface, no toast and no row — the same bound this entry's parent had, and the same reason it is P3
  - **The live population empties by retention at about 14:11 today, not by any fix.** All 10 raw-JSON rows for this source were ingested **2026-08-17 14:11:00–14:21:03** and nothing has created one since; `log_entries` keeps 30 days, so the rows survive until **2026-09-16** but leave the **7-day** `current` window this afternoon, classify `GONE`, and stop producing recommendations. Measure this entry before ranking it, or it reads as zero-population for the same reason `SNAG-LOG-010` was ranked last
  - **The live population emptied on 2026-08-28, three weeks early and by the first of the two fixes this entry names.** `SNAG-LOG-008`'s backfill re-derived those ten rows' messages, so the nine signatures that agreed past the cap are now `alert_raised` and one other: measured either side, **9 of 55 → 0 of 50** sharing a capped prefix, and the roll-up row that listed 7 identical members is gone. **The entry stays open and its check still reports `still holds`**, because that check reproduces the mechanism on a synthetic specimen rather than counting the live rows — deliberately, by this entry's own last bullet. What closed is the *instance*; what survives is that any cap is defeated by two records differing past it, which is the second candidate fix and is still unbuilt
  - **The stated cost is wrong, and the surface it excludes is the one with a row and a toast** (measured 2026-08-29 by Session 122). *"A GET advice surface, no toast and no row"* is true of `log_actions`; `log_signature.alert_title` cuts the same signature at a budget of 196–221 characters and **is** the dedup key, the set-based resolve's key and the tray's `{severity}:{title}` fingerprint. Two faults agreeing past it are one row, one fingerprint and one toast — `SNAG-AGENT-005`'s masking defect at the surface that entry exists to protect, reached from the other side
  - **Measured on this entry's own population, recovered rather than re-created.** `SNAG-LOG-008`'s backfill has rewritten `message`, but `raw_line` holds the journalctl record verbatim, so the pre-backfill signatures are recoverable exactly as `read_journal` composed them: **39 distinct signatures collapsed to 21 alert titles, four of them covering 2, 2, 2 and 16 distinct faults**. The sixteen are `warning` — stored, counted, not raised — and one pair is `error` and did raise
  - **The live margin is one fault wide.** Of 50 signatures on the box **2** are cut at all and none collide, so this half's population is empty by the same last-bullet rule as the rest of the entry. The surviving cut is a Python traceback whose first 211 characters are starlette's `lifespan` frame — boilerplate shared by every lifespan-time failure, which is the class `schema_guard` raises
  - **The alert half is FIXED 2026-08-29 by Session 122; the roll-up half stays open.** A cut title carries `SIGNATURE_DIGEST_CHARS` (8) hex characters of the whole signature's SHA-256. It is a **per-row pure function**, which is exactly what this entry says the divergence-aware cap cannot be — so the fix that is impossible one level down is available one level up, and only there. Only a *cut* title is stamped, so no open row's fingerprint moved: **0** open rows carried one (3 all-time, all resolved). `hashlib`, not builtin `hash`, which `PYTHONHASHSEED` salts per process and would make the aggregator and `log_trends` disagree about one fault across a restart
  - **What was pinning it**: `test_title_fits_the_column` asserted the cut was *marked* and never that it stayed *distinguishing* — two properties, one bought, green for the life of the module. Five tests replace it and only one goes red against the pre-fix code; the other four were falsified against mutations of the **fix**
  - **Check**: <!--check:capped_signature_collides--> `sysadmin-check-snags` — refuted when two signatures that agree past the cap stop rendering as one. **Reproduced, never counted**: this entry's population is empty at the live endpoint by its own last bullet, so the check drives the real `recommend()` over a probe pair instead — grouped, where the roll-up's member lines are compared, and apart, where two rows' titles are. The two halves are reported separately because they close to different fixes, and the probe's shared prefix is derived from `SIGNATURE_DETAIL_CHARS` so that raising the cap — the remedy this entry argues against — cannot read as one

- [P3] SNAG-LOG-011: **a deleted route still answers `200`, because `GET /api/logs/{source}` is a catch-all** (2026-08-24, opened by Session 69, **fixed 2026-08-24** by Session 75)
  - **Symptom**: Session 69 removed `GET /api/logs/summary` and `/summary/history` with the producer behind them. `curl localhost:8500/api/logs/summary` still returns `200` with `{"source": "summary", "entries": [], "count": 0}` — the `/{source}` route matching `summary` as though it were a log source
  - **So a caller is told "no summaries" where it should be told "gone".** That is `ports_checked`'s rule from the wrong side: zero-because-clean served as the same answer as zero-because-the-route-no-longer-exists. `/summary/history` correctly 404s, because the catch-all takes one path segment
  - **No live consumer**, measured: the string appears only in `SYSADMIN-SERVICE-SPEC.md`, never in `sysadmin_tray/`. The doc was corrected in the same sitting and points here
  - **It is a general property, not a one-off.** Any single-segment path under `/api/logs` that is added and later removed acquires this behaviour, and `/{source}` has been last in the router since it was written — which is what makes it work at all, so it cannot simply move
  - **The cheap fix is an explicit `410 Gone`** for the two retired paths, declared above the catch-all. The honest question underneath is whether `/{source}` should require the source to be a *declared* source, which would make every unknown segment a 404 and remove the class
  - **FIXED 2026-08-24 — both halves, and the entry's split between them was right.** Two `410 Gone` tombstones sit above the catch-all and `/{source}` now validates against the declared sources. Verified live on the daemon after a restart: `/summary` and `/summary/history` both `410`, `/kernel` and `/sysadmin.service` `200`, `/not-a-source` and `/alfred` both `404`
  - **The interesting part is the validator's key, and it is not one field.** `log_entries.source` holds the **unit** for a journal source and the **name** for a file source — `_read_journal_source` and `_read_log_file` stamp different things — so the rule lives in `services.stored_source_name`, mirroring the ingestion loop's dispatch. Written from the producer rather than from the data on purpose: every declared source here is `type: journalctl`, so a rule derived from the live table would have omitted the file branch and stayed green until the first file source was declared, at which point the route would 404 its own rows
  - **And the set is built from both configuration files.** `kernel` is declared in config.yaml because it belongs to no service, and it is **451,319 of the 451,569 rows** in `log_entries`. A set built from services.yaml alone passes every fixture on this box and rejects 99.9 % of the data — so `LogAggregatorAgent._sources` was lifted to `services.composed_log_sources` and shared, rather than the route restating it
  - **`410` rather than `404` for the two retired paths**, because "was a route and was removed" and "never was a route" are different states — `ports_checked`'s rule one status code up. `/summary/history` already 404'd and is named anyway so the pair answers with one voice
  - **Passing a source *name* was the same defect one level down**, and the entry did not name it: `/api/logs/alfred` returned an empty list, indistinguishable from a quiet service. It now 404s with the fifteen declared units in the detail
  - **The route had no tests at all**, which is how a route describing a dropped table stayed green through the sitting that dropped it. Eleven added, each falsified against the behaviour it replaces; the one worth naming is **ordering** — declaring the tombstone *below* the catch-all produces the same `200` as deleting it, so only a behavioural test can tell a future alphabetical sort from a working fix
  - **Cost, stated rather than implied**: a source removed from services.yaml keeps 30 days of rows this route no longer serves. Empty population today — all 9 distinct values in `log_entries.source` are declared — and `GET /api/logs/recent?source=` still reaches them, which is the surface for history

- [P4] SNAG-LOG-014: **two journal records are stored twice, because a resume boundary was re-read nineteen minutes before the fix for that was written** (2026-08-28, opened by Session 116)
  - **Symptom**: <!--check:duplicate_ingest_residue-->`sysadmin.service` holds **2** pairs of rows sharing a `logged_at` to the microsecond and a `message`, and they are the only duplicate pairs in `log_entries` at all — measured across every source, 2 and 2. `GET /api/logs/trends` therefore counts `alert_raised` two occurrences higher than the number of records that actually occurred
  - **Mechanism, dated rather than reasoned.** `format: json` was committed at **17:53:33** on 2026-08-17 and deployed by a restart at **19:50:19**; `SNAG-LOG-007`'s boundary close (`_is_unstored`, `stored_at_floor`) landed at **20:09:44**, *nineteen minutes after that restart*. So the restart's `_resume_floor` opened a window at its own newest stored second — `journalctl --since` is inclusive and `since_timestamp` truncates to whole seconds — and both records at `14:21:03` were re-read and stored again. The second copy arrived under the new declaration, so it was stored unwrapped
  - **Not caused by `SNAG-LOG-008`'s backfill and not visible without it.** Before the repair the twins carried different `message` values (one envelope, one fragment) and so were different signatures that hid each other; the backfill made them identical, which is the only view in which the pair is visible. `SNAG-LOG-004`'s ordering a fourth time — a fix that widens what a monitor can see is a regression surface for whatever reads it
  - **Nothing is creating new ones.** The mechanism was closed at 20:09:44 that evening and the population has been 2 ever since. It is residue from a fixed entry, not a live defect, which is why this is P4
  - **Deliberately not deleted in the sitting that found it, and that judgement stands.** Two rows are two rows; hand-deleting rows from a monitor's own history to correct an off-by-two is worse than the two. Re-measured 2026-08-29 by Session 123 and nothing below overturns the remedy — what it overturns is the *cost*
  - **The cost bullet named one surface and one endpoint's count, and both halves were the smaller one** (corrected 2026-08-29, driven through the real pipeline against the live table in a rolled-back transaction rather than reasoned about). `build_trend_report`'s own docstring says the advice *must* be computed off the same report the trend serves, so that the two surfaces cannot disagree about whether a signature is new — which makes an error in the grouped counts reach both **by construction**. Measured, stored against true:
    - `GET /api/logs/trends` moves **four** figures, not one: `previous` 23→21, `total` 91→89, `ratio` 2.96→3.24, and `sources[].previous_warnings` for `sysadmin.service` 28→26
    - `GET /api/logs/actions` is **not named by the entry at all** and carries the error in a **title**: the live row reads ``sysadmin.service fault up 3.0x — "alert_raised"`` against a true `3.2x`, with `23 occurrence(s) last window` against 21. `SNAG-LOG-010` is the entry that made a title this surface's row identity, so this is the same cap-and-count family reaching the field that family exists to protect
  - **And the duration is eighteen days rather than three, in the field the entry offers as the mitigation.** `previous` is filtered to a window and does converge on **2026-08-31** exactly as filed. `total` is `func.count()` with **no window filter at all**, so it stays 2 high until retention — which purges `log_entries` on `ingested_at`, not `logged_at`, putting both copies at **2026-09-16**. The bullet's *"an endpoint that already carries `total` as evidence"* names the one served figure that outlives the rest of the error by a fortnight. `total` is also the ordering tiebreaker after `current` (`log_trends.py`) and is printed in a `noise` row's detail (`log_actions.py`) — the first has an empty population today, measured: no other signature shares `current == 68` and the served ordering is identical in the counterfactual
  - **What it does *not* reach, measured rather than assumed.** The **alert family** is untouched: both copies are `warning` and `FAULT_SEVERITIES` is `("error", "critical")`, and the count in `details['occurrences']` is accumulated from the entries a run read rather than queried from the table — so no row, no fingerprint and no toast, which is the one place the entry's original framing is exactly right. The **weekly log review and the 06:00 briefing** are clean: the surge row ranks 6th of 8 and `build_facts_section` prints the top 3, `direction_phrase` runs on `error_delta`, and the facts section and the review prompt are byte-identical either side of the counterfactual. `GET /api/logs/stats` and `/api/logs/recent` cap `hours` at 168 and the rows are twelve days old
  - **What a fix would have to answer, if one is ever wanted** — and the untested half is now measured, which moved the deciding population to a different source: a general dedup on `(source, logged_at, message)` is safe only if a source cannot legitimately emit the identical message twice inside one microsecond. Across **235,230 retained rows and 9 sources**, *zero* groups share a `(source, logged_at)` while disagreeing about `message`, so the `message` component of that key is doing no work on this box at all. The tightest gap between two genuinely distinct records from one source is **3 µs**, and it is the **kernel** — not the millisecond of `alert_raised` writes this bullet named, which is 333× wider and the wrong source. A margin of three microseconds is thin and real, and it is thinnest where the volume is
  - **Check** (the marker is on the symptom bullet above): `sysadmin-check-snags` — and its key was corrected on 2026-08-29 for the reason this entry is the best evidence for. It grouped duplicates on `(source, logged_at, message)`, which is the column `SNAG-LOG-008`'s backfill **rewrote**, and these two rows were invisible for eleven days precisely because it had not yet: one copy held the envelope, one the fragment, so a `message`-keyed group could not see them. The *verdict* stays on the entry's own narrow key, because a wider one admits two distinct records coinciding in a microsecond and would hold `match` open after the real pair had aged out — the calendar keeping an entry alive, which is the mirror of the reading this check refuses. The **"is it happening elsewhere"** limb and the note move to the record's own identity, `(source, logged_at)`: that limb reaches no verdict, and it is the one whose blindness would cost something, since a second occurrence needs a restart and a restart is when a declaration changes. A wide count above the narrow one is named in the note as this entry's own pre-backfill shape

- [P4] SNAG-CFG-003: **the reminder ceiling is guarded by a test, and a SIGHUP installs a config no test has seen** (2026-08-28, opened by Session 119)
  - **Symptom**: `tests/test_config_defaults.py::TestTheLoudRungEndsBeforeItIsRestated` asserts that `service_discovery.scan_interval_hours` + `estate_judge.poll_interval_hours` stays under the tray's `reminder_hours`, which is what bounds `SNAG-ESTATE-009`'s loud rung at 7 h against a 24 h restatement. It runs when the suite runs. `POST /api/sysadmin/reload` and `SIGHUP` install a new `AppConfig` and re-time the scheduler from the file on disk, so an edit that puts the sum at 25 takes effect on the running daemon with nothing having judged it coherent
  - **The reload reports the wrong axis, and that is the point rather than an oversight.** `requires_restart` classifies a changed leaf by whether the process can deliver it live — `RESTART_ONLY`, `LIVE_AT_STARTUP`, or a `JobSpec`'s `config_paths` — and both leaves here are `JobSpec`-declared, so a 6 → 24 edit reloads cleanly and reports `jobs_retimed`, which is *true*. Nothing in that machinery asks whether the new values are coherent **with each other**; it is a delivery report, not a validation
  - **`scripts/claude-precommit.sh` does not run pytest** — it runs `check-migrations.sh` and lints — so the commit is not the moment either. The realistic path is still covered, because this box's config lives in the checkout and a sitting runs the suite at Step 4; what is not covered is the gap `SNAG-DB-005` is the 23-hour example of, where the box and the checkout disagree
  - **Population is empty and measured, not assumed**: the shipped sum is **7** against a `reminder_hours` of 24, a margin of 3.4×, and `service_discovery.scan_interval_hours` has read 6 since the agent was written. Nothing has ever reloaded a violating config here
  - **P4, on this file's precedent**: `SNAG-LOG-014` is P4 for being residue rather than a live defect; this is a *reachable* hole with no reachable population and no observation behind it, which is the same distance from a live defect in the other direction. Ranked rather than argued, and left for the owner
  - **What a fix would have to answer, if one is ever wanted**: whether `reload.py` grows a *semantic* verdict beside its delivery report — and if so, where the rule lives. Putting the inequality in production code makes the daemon a reader of `notifications.tray.reminder_hours`, which `may_quieten_in_place` rule 3 refuses for the backend on the grounds that the tray owns that policy; the test is free of that objection precisely because a test is not the backend. So the cheap fix is the one the rule forbids, and the honest one is a new question about who may read the tray's leaf
  - **Re-measured 2026-08-29 by Session 124, and the mechanism is exactly as filed.** Driven at the real `reload_configuration` with a real `Scheduler` and an injected `sync_jobs` — the daemon's shape, not the documented no-syncer fallback, which would have reported the entry refuted by its own harness: `ok=True`, `requires_restart=[]`, `jobs_retimed=['service_discovery_scan']`, the scheduler moved to `interval[1 day]`, and the installed sum read back as **25** against a `reminder_hours` of 24. The shipped margin is 7/24 = **3.43×** as stated, and `claude-precommit.sh` runs `check-migrations.sh` and lints with no pytest. <!--check:reload_unjudged_config-->
  - **The cost is the smaller half at the *installer*, and the fix bullet below is aimed at 2 of 131.** The entry scopes the unjudged install to `SIGHUP` and the POST. Over the 30 days the journal reaches, this box logged **2 reloads, 122 daemon starts and 7 tray starts** — a restart reads the same file with the same absence of a verdict and is **61× more frequent**, and it is the remedy every other check here recommends. The lifespan's one verdict is `verify_schema_revision()`, about the schema; nothing between it and `apply_jobs` asks whether the values cohere. So a semantic verdict grown in `reload.py` alone would leave the commonest installer exactly as it is — and `schema_guard` is the standing precedent for a *boot-time* verdict, which the entry does not weigh
  - **The cost is the smaller half at the *guard*, and this one is 1 of about thirty.** The suite holds roughly thirty live coherence assertions over the shipped `config.yaml` and `services.yaml` across nine test files, every one of them suite-only and every one installed by the same paths. Three concern the same quietening ladder as this entry. Driven rather than counted for the sharpest of them: setting the log source's `format` to `text` while `service.log_format` still reads `json` reloads with `ok=True` and `services_changed=['sysadmin-service']`, leaving the two declarations disagreeing — which returns `SNAG-LOG-003`'s 252-character JSON titles, the defect Session 64 shipped the declaration to end
  - **And the smaller half at the *input*: the entry names one of the inequality's three leaves.** Lowering `notifications.tray.reminder_hours` to 6 breaks the same ceiling and the reload reports **nothing at all** — `AppConfig` holds `mute_services` alone under `notifications.tray`, so no leaf is even seen to have moved; and its installer is a **tray restart**, since the tray reads its config once in `app.py` and has no reload path. Setting `service_discovery.enabled: false` breaks the property *harder* while the sum stays coherent, which the guard's own third class states in writing and this entry does not carry
  - **P4 is unmoved.** Every measurement above widens the blast radius and none of them finds a population: the shipped file is coherent, all ~30 relations hold, and nothing has ever reloaded or booted a violating config here. What changed is that the entry's own fix bullet is now known to be aimed at the rarest installer
  - **Found**: 2026-08-28 by Session 119, checking what actually executes the guard it had just built

- [P3] SNAG-LOG-012: **`strip_markdown` leaves inline backticks, so the model's code spans reach the briefing verbatim** — estate-lib's, **delegated** (2026-08-24, opened by Session 69)
  - **Symptom, from the first stored log review on 2026-08-24**: dria-agent-a-3b wrapped every service name in backticks (`` `sysadmin.service` ``) and `strip_markdown` passed them through. Driven directly: `strip_markdown('a `code` and **bold** and # head')` returns `` 'a `code` and bold and # head' `` — bold removed, backticks kept, and a mid-line `#` kept
  - **The narrative reaches a briefing section as plain text**, so the backticks are rendered literally by whatever displays it. Cosmetic, and it is the third markdown form this model has emitted against an explicit instruction not to
  - **It is not this repository's to fix.** `sysadmin/core/text.py` re-exports `estate.text`; `inspect.getsourcefile` puts the implementation at `~/projects/estate-manager/lib/estate/text.py`. Patching it from here would be a copy that drifts, which is the exact failure that module's own comment records (estate-manager ADR-0006)
  - **Routed as a recommendation, not a ruling** (estate rule: a question routed to the owner is answered by the owner). Every consumer here wants the same behaviour, so the recommendation is to strip inline code spans in the library rather than to add a second pass in any caller
  - **The consumers are three, not two** (corrected 2026-08-26 by Session 91, measured rather than counted). This bullet read "Both consumers here — `files/review.py` and `monitor/log_review.py`"; `monitor/health_review.py` was written on **2026-08-25**, the day *after* this entry was filed, and calls the same function into the same briefing. The check below re-measures the callers on every run rather than restating this number, because a count written into a body bullet is what went stale in a day
  - **Check**: <!--check:code_spans_survive--> `sysadmin-check-snags` — refuted when the strip stops leaving a code span, and `unknown` when it stops stripping anything at all. **The fourteenth check, and the first that is *pre-staged* against another repository's fix rather than a read of their tree**: `estate-lib` is an **editable** install here, so `strip_markdown` resolves into estate-manager's working tree and this flips to `mismatch` on the next run after they commit, with nothing synced and nobody told. The tenth and eleventh checks shell into their venv to drive their code; this one needs no cross-repo access at all
  - **The obvious probe reports `match` against a function that strips nothing**, so the specimen carries four **controls** the entry itself recorded as removed (heading, bullet, ordered item, bold) beside the code spans. A surviving backtick means nothing on its own — `` '`' in strip_markdown('a `x`') `` is `True` for the identity function — so a control that survives is `unknown` rather than evidence for this entry
  - **A partial fix is `mismatch` with the residue named, and the residue already has a name here.** The specimen carries a doubled fence beside the single one because `SNAG-DOCS-005` closed on exactly that distinction one module over on 2026-08-26: the naive `` `[^`]+` `` strips the single fence and *leaks* the doubled one, which is why `snag_claims.CODE_SPAN_RE` closes on a run of the same length. Driven both ways rather than asserted — the naive pattern gives `mismatch` naming the leak, the same-length pattern gives a clean `mismatch`
  - **This entry is one of the five the `SNAG-ESTATE-014` note says cannot carry a check**, and that is the second time the rule has been broken (see that note, corrected the same day). Being delegated is what makes a check *worth* writing — the closing move happens in a tree nothing here watches — and this one shows it need not cost a cross-repo read either
  - **What the live run corrected in the check itself**: the first drive reported **five** callers, two of them the check's own probe, which inflated the count *and* made the "nothing calls it" limb unreachable — a probe counting itself is `ops_claims` rule 3's pin searching a region containing its own marker, reached from the other side
  - **Deliberately not bundled**: a cross-repo write is committed on its own and announced

- [P3] SNAG-LOG-001: **one incident produces four recommendations, because systemd narrates a crash in four lines** (2026-08-17, opened by Session 59; symptom re-measured as six by Session 67; **FIXED 2026-08-18 by Session 68**)
  - **Symptom, from the first live run of `GET /api/logs/actions`**: the 2026-08-12 mosquitto core dump yields **four** `new_signature` rows — `Failed to start Mosquitto MQTT Broker daemon.`, `Process N (mosquitto) of user N dumped core.`, `mosquitto.service: Failed with result 'core-dump'.` and `mosquitto.service: Main process exited, code=dumped, status=N/SEGV`. Each is a genuinely distinct fault signature and each is correct; together they are one crash, and 4 of the 13 rows on the page describe it
  - **Why it is not a dedup bug**: `log_signature.py` rule 1 exists precisely because a source-level key let the Bluetooth storm mask an RCU stall. Merging these four would need to know they are causally related, and nothing does — they share a unit and a minute, which is also true of four independent faults during a bad minute
  - **Why it is not simply a cap either**: the family already has the shape (`attention_max_rows`, `port_breach_max_rows` — collapse above N, take the loudest rung). A cap would hide the fourth row without saying the four were one thing, which is the roll-up defect `SNAG-ESTATE-001` spent a session removing: a count cannot name anything. The interesting fix is a *correlation* rule — same unit, same window, one of them a `Failed to start`/`Main process exited` line systemd emits about another — and that is a rule nobody has measured
  - **Cost is bounded**: this is a GET advice endpoint, not an alert family, so it produces no toasts. It costs the reader's attention on one page
  - **Found**: 2026-08-17 by Session 59, by reading the endpoint's first real output rather than its fixtures
  - **The symptom is six, not four, and the proposed rule would miss two of them** (measured 2026-08-17 by Session 67, against the purged table — the first time these counts have been free of duplicate inflation). The same incident emits **six** rows spanning **two units** inside **349 ms**: mosquitto's four at 12:32:51.283926–.317106, then `estate-broker-provision.service: Failed with result 'exit-code'.` and `Failed to start Assert the estate MQTT dynsec schema (roles, clients).` at .632421 and .632608. The oneshot depends on the broker, so the crash took it down — one causal incident, two units
  - **So "same unit, same window" is the wrong shape for the rule this entry proposes.** A correlation keyed on the unit collapses mosquitto's four and leaves the provisioner's two standing as an unrelated second fault, which is the more misleading output: the reader is told a dependency failed *and* separately that a broker crashed. The relation that actually holds is systemd's own dependency graph — and **nothing in this repository reads it today**, which was checked rather than assumed: `scan.py` parses `WorkingDirectory`, `ExecStart`, `Restart=`, `RestartSec=`, `StartLimit*` and `[Install]`, and no `Requires=`/`After=`/`BindsTo=` anywhere. The directives sit in files the sweep already opens as text, so the *declared* graph is cheap; the **effective** graph is what `systemctl list-dependencies` resolves, and reaching for that would cost `scan.py` its no-subprocess promise, which Session 46 re-verified and which is load-bearing. Whether the declared graph is enough for this one relation is the thing to measure first
  - **The share is now the argument for doing it.** Post-purge the endpoint serves 24 recommendations and **6 of them are this one incident** — a quarter of the page, where the entry recorded 4 of 13. It does not self-clear and it recurs on every multi-line crash
  - **FIXED 2026-08-18 by Session 68 — and the declared graph was not merely sufficient, it was cheaper than this entry assumed.** `sysadmin/units/scan.py` gains `relations` on `UnitFile` and `declared_relations()`; `sysadmin/monitor/log_actions.py` gains `group_incidents()`, which collapses first sightings that share a unit **or a declared systemd dependency** inside `INCIDENT_WINDOW_SECONDS`. Live: `GET /api/logs/actions` went **24 → 11**, the specimen's six became one row titled `New incident: mosquitto.service then estate-broker-provision.service`, naming all six signatures and emitting one `journalctl -u … -u …` that was run and works
  - **The entry said the effective graph might be needed and it is not, but the measurement that settled it is not the one this entry proposed.** It framed the choice as declared-versus-effective and worried about `scan.py`'s no-subprocess promise. The real question turned out to be *which directories*: `mosquitto.service` is a packaged unit the sweep excludes as distro-owned and whose file lives in `/usr/lib/systemd/system`, which the sweep never walks — so the obvious move was to widen the walk. **Measured: parsing `/usr/lib` as well reads 629 further unit files and yields zero further relations between the fourteen declared log sources.** A relation is declared by the unit that *depends*, and on this estate that unit is always the hand-written one. The sweep's existing two directories are enough, at 16× less I/O and with the no-subprocess promise never in question
  - **The window is not the rule, and the live window proves it.** `alfred-backend.service` failed **1.2036 s** after the mosquitto crash — inside any usable window — for an entirely unrelated reason (`asyncpg.exceptions.CannotConnectNowError: the database system is starting up`), and `sportsanalyser-backend.service` failed **2.9 s before** it. Both are excluded by the graph and neither could be excluded by a clock. Driven as a counterfactual rather than argued: promoting alfred-backend's real signature to a first sighting at its real offset leaves the group at 6, and forging one edge into the graph takes it to 7
  - **The constant is derived and the derivation is a gap rather than a number.** Across the 21 first sightings the live trend holds, separations are bimodal with nothing in between: every genuinely-one-incident pair lands inside **349 ms**, and the nearest genuinely-two-incidents pair is **64.4 s** apart (`sysadmin.service`'s startup `api.auth_token is not set` warning, then the aggregator's alert burst a minute later). Every value between produces identical output, so `INCIDENT_WINDOW_SECONDS = 5.0` — the geometric midpoint — sits three orders of magnitude from anything it could get wrong
  - **The causality in the previous bullet was backwards, and the journal says so.** Session 67 wrote that mosquitto "took the `estate-broker-provision` oneshot with it". systemd started the oneshot **2 ms after** mosquitto had already failed (`12:32:51.284877` then `.286928`), because the declared relation is `Wants=`, which does not propagate failure. The provisioner then failed on its own connect — `mosquitto_ctrl dynsec listClients: Error: Bad file descriptor.` One causal incident either way, and the fix does not depend on the direction; but the mechanism written down was not the mechanism
  - **The whole window is a boot, which nothing had noticed.** Boot `0` begins at **12:32:39**, twelve seconds before the crash, and every failure in the minute either side is a boot-time startup failure. That is why a same-window rule is so dangerous here and why the graph is doing the work
  - **Byproduct, measured rather than claimed**: the same rule collapses `sysadmin.service`'s raw-JSON rows (`SNAG-LOG-008`) from **10 recommendations to 3** — seven of them are one `log_aggregator` run's alerts inside 1.7 ms. That is 6 of the 13 rows removed; the specimen is 5 and the remaining 2 are a second sysadmin burst. It does not close `SNAG-LOG-008`, which is about the signatures being unreadable, not about how many rows they occupy

- [P2] SNAG-LOG-002: **the noise recommendation has an empty population on this box — and the reason is the kernel, not this service** (2026-08-17, opened by Session 59; **cause corrected 2026-08-17 by Session 60**; ceiling half fixed by Session 62; **FIXED 2026-08-17 by Session 63**)
  - **Fixed 2026-08-17 by Session 63, and the two live rows are the falsification.** `log_trends._confidence` was `if coverage.runs_truncated > 0: return LOW` — binary, so one catch-up read pinned the whole report for fourteen days. It is now `truncated_fraction > TRUNCATION_LOW_FRACTION` (0.05) over the **instrumented** reads. Driven against the live database after the change: confidence `medium`, **25 recommendations including the 2 `noise` rows** the family was written to produce — both Bluetooth firmware signatures at 39,921 occurrences, exactly Tier 2's scoped example
  - **The handoff's stated mechanism was wrong, and measuring it is what made the change safe.** It named "the post-restart catch-up read, which `_resume_floor()` sizes by daemon downtime". `_resume_floor()` returns the newest stored `logged_at` **for that unit**, so it sizes the window by *how long since that source last stored a row* — which for a quiet source is days, not the two seconds a `systemctl restart` takes. That is why the 16 non-storm truncations each name **four or five sources at once**: measured, every one of them is the first `log_aggregator` poll after a restart, ~62 s after `Started SysAdmin…`, and a source logging one warning a week is read a week back on every restart
  - **Which is also why Session 62's ceiling fix reaches them, against the expectation that no ceiling could.** With `-p`, the 500-entry budget is spent on *storable* entries, and a week-long catch-up window on a quiet source holds about one. Live proof on the same box, same day, same restart shape: **13:17:05 restart → the 13:18:08 poll truncated 4 sources** (pre-`-p`); **14:10:58 restart → the 14:12:00 poll clean** (post-`-p`). So the population this gate was written for is smaller than either the entry or the handoff supposed — the gate is what stops the *history* of it suppressing the family for a fortnight
  - **The denominator was wrong and is fixed in the same change.** `_trend_coverage` counted the numerator over runs carrying `details['truncated_sources']` and the denominator over **every** run in the window. That field first appears 2026-08-12 17:31, so 10,724 of the window's 17,730 runs could not have reported truncation. Measured: **120 of 7,006 instrumented (1.71 %)**, not 120 of 17,730 (0.68 %) — 2.5x, self-correcting as those runs age out, which is precisely why leaving it was not an option: a number wrong today and right next week is one nobody re-checks
  - **Lowering the gate is not what happened, and the asymmetry is what makes a threshold legitimate at all.** A truncated read *drops* entries, so it can only make a count too **low** — a `noise` row argues a signature is loud, and a floor cannot be undercut by data that is missing. That is rule 4's own `NEW` asymmetry ("a gap can hide a fault, never invent one") one step further. What the threshold bounds is not the volume error but the chance a depressed *current* window moves a genuine `SURGED` signature into the noise-eligible `STEADY` band. Both live rows are `RETURNED` with `previous = 0`, so no ratio is computed for either and nothing is distorted
  - **`HIGH` is untouched**, deliberately: it still means nothing was lost and nothing was missed. Only the floor beneath it moved, so a report that loses a little is `MEDIUM` — which `_is_noise_candidate` already treats as good enough, since it only ever tested for `LOW`
  - **`TRUNCATION_LOW_FRACTION` is invented and says so**, on `RATIO_MIN_COUNT`'s terms, but with the numbers to hand: the fortnight reads 1.7 % and the storm's own day reads 104 of 1,434 (**7.3 %**), so 5 % puts a fortnight containing one bounded storm at `MEDIUM` and a window that is mostly storm at `LOW`
  - **`truncated_fraction` fails closed on not-knowing** — `schema_guard`'s posture, not `collation.py`'s. A caller reporting truncation with no `runs_instrumented` gets `1.0`, so every pre-existing test and every unmigrated caller keeps the binary behaviour. That is also why the guards had to be falsified: **all 1,984 tests passed on the first run after the change**, because the old fixtures set no denominator
  - **Falsified before being trusted.** Setting `TRUNCATION_LOW_FRACTION = 0.0` restores the binary rule *exactly* — it is the limit case, not a replacement — and breaks precisely the four new proportional tests and nothing else
  - **Half fixed 2026-08-17 by Session 62, and the fix named below was refuted before it was written.** Per-source confidence was driven against the live database through the real `_build_trend_report` → `recommend()`: it produces **zero** noise rows. The entire noise-eligible population on this box is two kernel signatures at 39,920 apiece, and kernel carries **103 of the 120** truncated runs, so per-source confidence gates the only candidates on the only heavily-truncating source. The eight sources it liberates have a loudest signature of **54**, against `NOISE_MIN_OCCURRENCES = 100` — under the gate across the whole 40-day retained history
  - **It would also have failed silently, on a seam this repository has already documented.** `agent_runs.details['truncated_sources']` names sources by their `services.yaml` **name** (`alfred`, `sports_analyser`); `log_entries.source` and `SignatureTrend.source` hold the **unit** (`alfred-backend.service`). `kernel` is the one string in both, so the obvious join reads all eight non-kernel sources as untruncated and kernel as truncated — wrong in both directions, and green. `_log_source_scopes` carries the same warning one function over
  - **The denominator was wrong too.** `details['truncated_sources']` first appears on the run at **2026-08-12 17:31**; 33,090 earlier runs have no such key. So it is 120 truncated of **6,974 instrumented** runs, not 119 of 10,063, and "kernel truncated in only one week" is an artefact of when the field landed rather than a fact about kernel — the July weeks at 87k/69k/116k Bluetooth lines predate it entirely
  - **What was actually wrong was that the ceiling counted the wrong lines, and that is fixed.** `read_journal` applied `severity_filter` in **Python, after** `-n 500` had already bounded the raw journalctl output. Measured across the storm (12:33–19:11): **203,042 raw kernel lines carrying 81,216 storable ones — 40 %**. Per minute, a median of **510 raw against a ceiling of 500**, so **208 of 210 storm minutes truncated**; the 100 instrumented storm minutes produced **103 truncated reads, one per poll**. Passing `-p` to journalctl makes the budget 100 % useful and stores an **identical multiset** (verified line by line), so the effective ceiling rose 2.5× with no change to `max_entries_per_read` and no change to what is stored
  - **This does not close the entry, and the remaining half is the binary flag rather than the per-source one.** Catch-up reads survive: `_resume_floor()` sets the window to how long the daemon was down, so one restart behind a backlog truncates however high the ceiling is. Since `_confidence` is `runs_truncated > 0` over a 14-day window, **one such read still pins the whole report `LOW` for fourteen days.** The candidate is therefore proportional-rather-than-binary confidence — which the entry already names below and already says should not be done to unblock a demo, so it wants its own sitting and an argument about honesty rather than about a number
  - **Re-measured 2026-08-17 by Session 61, and the composition is estate-wide rather than two services.** Session 60 named "kernel 103, sysadmin-service 14", which reads as a two-service problem and is 2 of **9** sources. Live over 7 days: **119 runs truncated of 10,063**, and per source `kernel 103, mosquitto 15, sysadmin-service 14, sports_analyser 13, venture-assistant 11, estate-manager-api 3, alfred 3, postgresql 1, alfred-frontend 1` — **164 source-truncations across 119 runs**, so some runs truncate several. Eight of the nine sources truncate occasionally
  - **This strengthens rather than weakens the per-source-confidence candidate.** A global binary flag is being tripped by nine different sources on their own schedules, so the report is `LOW` far more reliably than any one storm explains, and "wait for the 08-12 spike to leave the window" does not recover it. `details['truncated_sources']` already names the source per run; nothing aggregates it, and that aggregation is the whole fix
  - **Session 61's priority-half fix does not close this either**, and now for a measured reason rather than an inferred one: taking `sysadmin-service` to 0 leaves 105 runs truncated by eight other sources
  - **Symptom**: `GET /api/logs/actions` returns 13 recommendations and **zero** `noise` rows, though the two Bluetooth firmware signatures sit at 39,919 occurrences apiece — exactly Tier 2's scoped example. Forcing `confidence` to `HIGH` produces both rows correctly, so the family works and is simply unreachable
  - **Cause, and it is correct behaviour**: `log_actions` rule 4 suppresses every argument-from-a-count when confidence is `LOW`, and confidence is `LOW` because **118 runs in the window reported truncation**
  - **This entry's account of _which_ runs was wrong, corrected 2026-08-17 by Session 60 against `agent_runs`.** It said the 118 were "overwhelmingly `sysadmin-service` … on essentially every read". Measured over the same 7-day window: **kernel 103, mosquitto 14, sysadmin-service 14, sports_analyser 12, venture-assistant 11**, and 118 truncated runs out of **10,064** is **1.2 %**, not "essentially every". **104 of the 118 landed on one day**, 2026-08-12, as kernel reads — the Bluetooth firmware storm at 8.5 lines a second, which is a real ceiling nothing can remove
  - **So the fix named below would not have cleared this, and that is the more useful finding.** `log_trends._confidence` is `if coverage.runs_truncated > 0: return LOW` — **binary, not proportional**. Taking `sysadmin-service` from 14 truncated runs to 0 leaves 103 kernel ones and the report stays `low`. `SNAG-AGENT-008`'s volume half was fixed on 2026-08-17 anyway, on its own merits; this entry did not close with it
  - **What will actually happen**: the 08-12 spike falls out of the 7-day window around 2026-08-19 and confidence recovers **by itself**, then drops again on the next kernel storm. A family that is available only between storms is not available
  - **So one service's volume suppressing a family for every other source is still the shape**, but the service is the *kernel* and the volume is not a defect. Confidence is global to the report because the poll is global to the agent; there is no per-source confidence, and inventing one would need per-source truncation history, which `details['truncated_sources']` does carry but nothing aggregates
  - **The candidate fixes are therefore reordered.** Per-source confidence is now the **only** one that reaches the case, and the measurement above is what makes it worth the work: truncation on this box is normal for one source and rare for the rest, which is precisely the discrimination a global flag cannot make. A second candidate is to make the gate proportional rather than binary — 1.2 % of runs is not the same evidential state as 100 % — but that is a change to a rule about honesty and should not be made to unblock a demo
  - **Still deliberately not fixed by lowering the gate**, for the reason below, which the correction above strengthens rather than weakens
  - **Deliberately not fixed by lowering the gate.** A noise recommendation is an argument that a count is stable; serving it off a count known to be missing data is the opposite of what rule 4 is for, and the first thing it would recommend silencing is a signature it cannot see the whole of
  - **Found**: 2026-08-17 by Session 59, when the endpoint's first live run produced none of the rows the session was written to produce

- [P1] SNAG-DB-004: **the nightly retention purge has deleted nothing since 2026-08-08, while logging six successful purges a night** (2026-08-17, **fixed same day**)
  - **Symptom, measured before the fix**: `sysadmin.log_entries` held 626,906 rows reaching back to 2026-07-09 — 39 days into a declared 30-day retention — and `retention_config.last_purged_at` read `2026-08-08 03:00` for every table that has ever been stamped, with `disk_reviews`, `project_reviews`, `reliability_scores` and `unit_audits` never stamped at all. The 03:00 journal for 2026-08-17 carries `retention_purged … "table": "log_entries", "deleted": 175018` and five more like it, none of which happened
  - **Cause**: `KEEP_LATEST_PER` used the literal string `"true"` for "the whole table is one entity", spliced into `SELECT DISTINCT ON (true) id … ORDER BY true, generated_at DESC`. PostgreSQL reads a bare constant in `ORDER BY` as an **ordinal position**, so this is `non-integer constant in ORDER BY` — a parse error, not a runtime one. Parenthesising does not help; the parser strips it. `true::boolean` and `(1=1)` both parse, and neither was needed in the end
  - **Why nine days**: `run_retention` was a **single transaction over twelve tables**, so the twelfth table's parse error discarded the eleven before it. Each table logs its rowcount *before* the commit, so the failure was quieter than the success — the journal reported work that then rolled back. The one honest signal was `last_purged_at` frozen at 2026-08-08, a column nothing reads, plus a nightly `scheduler_job_error` covered by `SNAG-AGENT-008` below
  - **Why 1,866 green tests missed it**: `tests/test_retention.py` mocked the session, so every statement was asserted as a **string** and none was ever parsed. One test asserted `KEEP_LATEST_PER["project_reviews"] == "true"` — it pinned the broken literal exactly. No stronger string assertion could have helped; the thing that was wrong was SQL validity
  - **Fixed 2026-08-17** by three changes that are only jointly sufficient. `WHOLE_TABLE = None` is a **sentinel taking its own branch** (`ORDER BY … LIMIT 1`), removing the construct rather than repairing it — `NOT IN` over an empty subquery is `TRUE`, so an empty table needs no special case. Statement construction moved to a pure `purge_statement()`, which exists so `test_purge_statements_parse` can hand every statement to PostgreSQL via `EXPLAIN` — no scheduler, no clock, no row, and it covers the whole map because the next bad statement will be a different one. And **one savepoint per table** (`SysAdminAgent._execute`'s rule, one domain over) so a bad table costs only itself, with `last_purged_at` stamped *inside* the savepoint so a failed table keeps its old value and the column finally means what its name says
  - **Verified live rather than only against fixtures**: the real statements driven against the real database inside a rolled-back transaction — all twelve tables purge, 207,566 rows due to go at the next 03:00 (`log_entries` 626,906 → 451,888, `alerts` 685,649 → 675,531, `service_health` 101,826 → 89,258). The two `WHOLE_TABLE` tables delete 0 there, so the keep-latest semantic was forced separately with `cutoff = now()`: 4 rows → 3 deleted, 1 left, and the survivor is the newest in both. Nothing was deleted; row counts re-read after rollback are unchanged
  - **Both new guards were falsified before being trusted**: with the pre-fix code path restored, `test_purge_statements_parse` and `test_no_bare_constant_reaches_an_order_by` both fail with production's exact message, `non-integer constant in ORDER BY`
  - **Found**: 2026-08-17 by Session 59, while measuring how far back `log_entries` reaches — a question asked to bound Session 27's week-on-week window, not to audit retention

- [P1] SNAG-AGENT-008: **this service cannot see its own errors — every line it writes is journald priority 6** (2026-08-17, **fixed 2026-08-17** — volume half by Session 60, priority half by Session 61)
  - **Symptom**: `sysadmin.service` is a declared journal source in `services.yaml` at `severity_filter: warning`, and `sysadmin.log_entries` holds **0 rows** for it over the last 7 days — across nine consecutive nights on which `run_retention` raised a traceback logged at Python level `ERROR` (`SNAG-DB-004` above). No alert was ever raised. `journalctl -u sysadmin -p warning --since "7 days ago"` returns 0 lines
  - **Cause**: the daemon writes structured JSON to **stdout**, and systemd stamps captured stdout `PRIORITY=6` regardless of the `"level"` inside the payload. `read_journal` filters on journald's `PRIORITY` field, so `PRIORITY_MAP` maps every line to `info`, which is below `warning` — the filter discards the lot. Measured over the two-minute window containing the 03:00 failure: **277 entries, all `PRIORITY=6`**, the traceback among them
  - **A second, independent cause of the same blindness — volume, and this half was FIXED on 2026-08-17 (Session 60)**: `sysadmin.service` emitted **673 journal lines per 5 minutes** (676 over a 30-minute sample — steady, not bursty), so a read could hit the `-n 500` ceiling. The cursor advances over every line read, **before** the severity filter (correctly — that rule exists so info-level noise does not strand the resume point), so unread errors older than the ceiling are skipped permanently. Fixing the priority alone would not be sufficient
  - **What this entry originally claimed about the volume was wrong in three ways, all found by measuring rather than re-reading it.** It said the ceiling is hit "essentially every time"; it is hit **on the first read after a restart and at no other moment**. It said the cause is "an access-log line per HTTP request at INFO into the journal, a choice nobody revisited"; the dominant causes were a *duplicated* line and a tray polling a tab nobody was looking at. And the composition was never measured before being asserted
  - **Cause 1 — every request was logged twice.** `configure_logging` clears the **root** handlers, which does not reach `uvicorn.access`: uvicorn's default dictConfig attaches a handler to that logger directly and sets `propagate = False`, so it survived untouched and emitted a plain-text line beside the middleware's JSON one. Measured over ten minutes: **662 plain against 640 JSON access lines**, and 662 − 640 is exactly the **22 `/health` polls** the middleware excludes and uvicorn's did not — so `_EXCLUDED_PATHS`, which is `SNAG-API-002`'s fix, **has never worked**. `tests/test_logging.py::TestRequestLoggingMiddleware::test_excludes_health_endpoint` asserted the exclusion by patching the *middleware's* logger, i.e. the one that was already honouring it, so no test could have caught the emitter that was not
  - **Cause 2 — the tray fanned out `/details` for a tab nobody was looking at.** `ServicesTab` is constructed eagerly at tray startup (`sysadmin_tray/app.py`) and wired to `status_updated` unconditionally, so it issued one `GET /api/sysadmin/services/{name}/details` per systemd-backed service on every status poll whether or not the dashboard had ever been opened — ~32 services every `tray.status_poll_seconds`. **1,160 of the 1,347 lines in ten minutes, 86 %.** `DashboardWindow`'s own docstring promised the opposite (*"no background polling when hidden"*) and every other tab keeps it; `LogsTab` stops its timer in `hideEvent` and this one had no timer to stop, which is how it escaped notice — the polling was not scheduled, it was inherited from a signal that fires anyway
  - **Why the volume bit at exactly one moment, and it is the two halves of this snag multiplying.** `_read_journal_source` falls back to `since = "5m ago"` only when there is no cursor **and** `_resume_floor` returns `None`. For `sysadmin-service` the floor is *always* `None`, because the priority half means 0 rows are ever stored — so the durable resume mechanism is permanently disabled, every restart re-reads a five-minute window, and five minutes at 673 lines overflows a 500-line ceiling. The two halves were filed as independent and are multiplicative: fixing **either** stops the truncation
  - **Fixed 2026-08-17** by removing both causes rather than raising the ceiling. `logging.getLogger("uvicorn.access").disabled = True` in `configure_logging` (disabled rather than re-levelled: uvicorn logs access at INFO and nothing else, so `setLevel(WARNING)` would be silence spelled indirectly); and `ServicesTab._on_status` gates the fan-out on `self.isVisible()`, with `refresh()` fetching details for cards already held so a tab shown after polls it ignored does not sit blank for a poll interval. The middleware is the copy kept, because only it carries `method`/`path`/`status`/`duration_ms` as structured fields
  - **Both guards were falsified against the restored pre-fix code before being trusted.** `test_access_line_is_not_emitted` fails with production's exact line, `127.0.0.1:53994 - "GET /health" 200`; four of the seven `test_services_tab.py` tests fail with the whole service list. The logging half was additionally driven against uvicorn's **real** `LOGGING_CONFIG` rather than a reconstruction of it: `dictConfig(LOGGING_CONFIG)` then `configure_logging` gives `INFO:     127.0.0.1:53994 - "GET /health HTTP/1.1" 200 OK` before and `''` after, with nothing rerouted to the root handler
  - **Priority half fixed 2026-08-17 (Session 61), and the entry's own statement of the trade-off was wrong.** It said the two unit-file remedies both need `sudo` and that reading the `"level"` key in `read_journal` is "the only one needing no unit-file edit". Measured: `systemctl show sysadmin.service -p SyslogLevelPrefix` already reads **`yes`**, because `SyslogLevelPrefix=` **defaults to true** in systemd. So the prefix remedy needs no unit edit and no `sudo` either — it sits on exactly the footing the entry credited only to the reader-side hack, while fixing the artefact rather than one consumer's view of it
  - **Verified against a transient unit before any code was written**, not read off the documentation: `<4>{…}` on stdout arrives as `PRIORITY=4` and journald **strips the prefix**, so `MESSAGE` is byte-identical to the unprefixed line and `log_signature`, `alert_title` and the stored `raw_line` need no change
  - **Why the producer and not the reader.** Parsing `"level"` inside `read_journal` leaves the journal itself lying: `journalctl -u sysadmin -p err` still prints nothing, and so does any `OnFailure=` hook or anyone reading by hand. It would also put a special case for **one** source into a reader serving fourteen, keyed on this application's log format — and `sysadmin.service` is the only JSON-writing journal source on this box (measured: `estate-manager-api` wrote 266 plain lines in six hours, alfred none), so the branch could never pay for itself
  - **`systemd.journal` was rejected on measurement, not argument**: `from systemd import journal` raises `ImportError` in the venv, so it is a new native dependency, and it replaces stdout-JSON with a different transport rather than repairing it
  - **The prefix is bound to the JSON format, and the gate is a precondition rather than a proxy.** A level prefix marks one line, and only the JSON formatter guarantees one line per record — `json.dumps` escapes newlines, so a traceback travels on the line whose level describes it. Under the text formatter the first line would be stamped `ERROR` and the body left at `info`: one fault split across two priorities, which is worse than the uniform `6` it replaced because it *looks* fixed
  - **A second emitter was in scope and it is the one carrying the errors.** `uvicorn.error` has no handler and propagates only as far as `uvicorn`, which keeps a plain-text stderr handler with `propagate = False` — exactly the shape `uvicorn.access` had. So `Exception in ASGI application` and the traceback of every unhandled 500 went out as plain text at `PRIORITY=6`, and no prefix on *this application's* logger could have reached them. **Rerouted, not silenced**, deliberately the opposite verb from its sibling three lines up in the same function: the access line duplicates a structured line the middleware already writes, so the second copy is waste; uvicorn's error line has no second copy anywhere, so silencing it would delete the only record an ASGI crash leaves
  - **Session 60's own guard was asserting the opposite, and passing.** `test_only_the_access_logger_is_silenced` claims `uvicorn.error` reaches the root handler; its fixture rebuilds `uvicorn.access` and **not** its parent, leaving `uvicorn` with no handler and `propagate = True`. Driven against uvicorn's real `LOGGING_CONFIG` on 2026-08-17, the record went to uvicorn's own handler as `ERROR:    address already in use` and the root buffer stayed **empty**. A green test asserting a decision the box does not implement is `test_excludes_health_endpoint`'s defect one logger over, shipped by the session that found it — so the new class drives the real `dictConfig` and the old test's docstring now says what it does not cover
  - **Verified live rather than only against fixtures, and without the `sudo` the deploy needs.** A transient user unit ran the real `configure_logging` and emitted four records; journald recorded `INFO→6`, `WARNING→4`, `ERROR`+traceback→`3`, `uvicorn.error→3`, and the **real `read_journal`** at `severity_filter: warning` returned **3 entries where it has always returned 0**, prefix stripped, cursor set. The round trip is pinned by a test against `journal.PRIORITY_MAP` for all five levels, because two maps that can disagree about one fact is this repository's recurring defect
  - **Both new guards were falsified against restored pre-fix code**: reverting the formatter fails 12 tests including every level case, and removing the uvicorn reroute fails exactly the two that name it
  - **Why this is the interesting half of `SNAG-DB-004`**: the alerting path was not merely unused, it was structurally unreachable. This repository's rule is that the monitor must not own the things it monitors — but it does monitor itself, via `self_monitor` and its own `services.yaml` entry, and that self-observation has a hole exactly where its own stack traces are
  - **What the entry said before it was fixed, kept because the premise was the interesting part**: "the candidate remedies are not equivalent and the choice is not obvious: `SyslogLevelPrefix=` plus a prefixing handler, `systemd.journal.JournalHandler`, or reading the `"level"` key out of the JSON payload in `read_journal` — the last being the only one needing no unit-file edit and therefore no `sudo`". Two of those three clauses were wrong, and one `systemctl show` settled both. A trade-off written from documentation rather than from the box sent the choice toward the weakest of the three
  - **Still owed: `sudo systemctl restart sysadmin`.** uvicorn serves start-time code, so `log_entries` holds 0 rows for this source until the daemon is restarted — the same restart the volume half has been waiting on since 2026-08-17. Both halves land together
  - **Found**: 2026-08-17 by Session 59, by asking why nine nights of `ERROR` produced no alert row — the question the retention fix raised rather than answered

- [P0] SNAG-LOG-004: **the log aggregator dies on the first traceback this daemon writes, permanently, once a minute** (2026-08-17, **found and fixed same day** by Session 64)
  - **Symptom**: `journalctl -o json` substitutes `null` for any field over ~4096 bytes unless `-a` is passed, and `read_journal` did not pass it. `MESSAGE` therefore returned `None`, `entry["message"][:5000]` in `LogAggregatorAgent._execute` raised `TypeError: 'NoneType' object is not subscriptable`, and the whole run failed — every source in it, not just the one that produced the line
  - **Self-sustaining, which is what makes it P0 rather than P1.** The run's failure is logged by `logger.exception("agent_run_failed")`, which is itself a >4096-byte line at `ERROR`. So the next poll reads *that*, gets `null`, and crashes again. Measured: **all 215 historic `agent_run_failed` lines are 12,837–12,845 bytes** and every one exceeds the cap. `log_entries` would take no rows from any of the 14 sources for as long as the daemon ran, and the `Log error:` family that watches the kernel storm would go blind — SNAG-DB-001's 39-hour blackout arriving by a fourth route
  - **Reachable only for a source that puts a long record on one line**, which on this box is this daemon alone: a Python traceback from any other service arrives as many short journal entries, while `JsonFormatter` folds `exc_info` into a single `MESSAGE`. That is why 40,228 `log_aggregator` runs have never failed
  - **Armed by the fix that preceded it, and not yet sprung.** These lines were stamped `PRIORITY=6` until the 14:10:58 restart, so `-p 4` excluded them; Session 61's level prefix is what made them reachable. Measured at the moment of the fix: **0 error lines and 146 clean `log_aggregator` runs since the restart** — the trap was live and untriggered
  - **Found by driving SNAG-LOG-003 against real rows rather than by looking for it**, which is the whole argument for doing so: the snag it was hiding behind is cosmetic and this one is a blackout
  - **Fixed 2026-08-17 (Session 64)** by `-a`, with `message_text()` behind it for the one shape `-a` introduces — a non-UTF-8 field, which `-a` renders as an array of byte values rather than as `null`. That branch has an **empty population** on this box (205,298 kernel records over seven days returned `str` for every one, with and without `-a`) and exists because the shape is journalctl's to choose
  - **It is a property of the JSON serialiser, not of journalctl's reading.** The same records print in full under the default text output, verified at 11,572 and 12,164 characters — so `journal_command`, the invocation `GET /api/logs/actions` hands a human, needed no change and was checked rather than assumed
  - **Verified live**: the same 12 kB `ERROR` line emitted into a transient user unit, read back through the real `read_journal` at 9,396 characters as `str`, sliced to 5,000 with no `TypeError`. Before the fix the identical probe returned `NoneType` and both `[:5000]` and `alert_title` raised
  - **Both guards falsified deliberately.** Removing `-a` breaks 4 tests; reverting `message_text()` to `data.get("MESSAGE", "")` breaks 2 — different tests, so the two halves cannot pass for each other's reasons

- [P2] SNAG-LOG-005: **the daemon's own failures now raise two alert families, and the journal one undercuts a threshold the other set deliberately** (2026-08-17, opened by Session 64, **FIXED 2026-08-17 by Session 65**)
  - **Symptom**: since `SNAG-AGENT-008`'s priority half and `SNAG-LOG-003`'s declaration, an agent failure produces `Log error: sysadmin-service — agent_run_failed` from `log_aggregator` reading the journal, *and* `<agent> agent failing` from `sysadmin/monitor/failures.py` reading `agent_runs`. One fault, two rows, two distinct tray `{severity}:{title}` fingerprints, two toasts
  - **The thresholds disagree, and that is the sharp end.** `failures.py` requires **two** consecutive failures and its docstring argues the rule out: *"One failure resolves itself on the next run — for `log_aggregator` that is a warning toast with a 60-second life, which is noise. Two consecutive means the fault survived a retry."* The journal path raises on the **first** line, so it announces exactly the event a sibling family decided was not worth announcing. The deliberate threshold is not overridden by argument, it is bypassed by a second producer
  - **This is the second-owner defect at a sixth scale**, and the first time this repository has created one rather than found one. The five existing instances are named in `CLAUDE.md`; the pattern is that a lifecycle needs one owner, and agent-run health has one already
  - **It could not exist before 2026-08-17.** `log_entries` held 0 rows for `sysadmin.service` across nine nights, so the journal family had no population. Session 61 gave it one and this session made it legible — the fix is what made the collision visible, which is the argument for it rather than against
  - **Not yet observed**, because there have been no `ERROR` lines since the 14:10:58 restart. The population is real though: **215 historic `agent_run_failed` lines**, all from the `sysadmin` agent
  - **Three candidate fixes, none costed.** (a) A `known_noise` entry for `(sysadmin.service, agent_run_failed)`, which quietens to `info` and is the mechanism already built for exactly this — but it silences a *legitimate* fault report rather than a noisy one, so the `reason` field would be recording a design decision as an operator judgement. (b) Exclude this daemon's own unit from the alert half of the log aggregator while still ingesting it, which is the monitor-must-not-own-what-it-monitors rule read one scale down and needs a statement of who *does* own it. (c) Narrow the log family's population to signatures no other family covers, which needs the set of covered signatures written down somewhere both can read — and a set maintained by hand is the `SNAG-CFG-001` shape
